The rest keeps one source for each fact — the request for where a task stands and what language it is in — and makes every "only once" of 03-flows a field of the profile rather than a hope about the model. — *Rejected:* the fallback of SPEC 7.5 as it stood; a `language` argument of `submit_task` beside the request's, which would leave the question of which is right; the limits of `next_task` in its input schema, and the parts of a task typed there, whose library would quote back what broke them (remark 36); the package in the payload, which a card is drawn from; a stale task shown as a wait over a task the child is still solving. — *Where it lives:* SPEC 3.4, 4.1, 5.9, 7.2, 7.3, 7.5, 11.2, 12.2, 12.5 and remarks 29 and 36; `docs/architecture/03-flows.md`, `docs/architecture/04-profile.md`; RUN.md (T44).

**R137. A part of a task sent as a string of its JSON is read for the JSON it holds.** `submit_task` takes the brief, the task and the self-check as whatever JSON arrives (R100). A part that arrives as a string holding valid JSON is read for that JSON, decoded into the same plain values as a part sent as JSON, so that the checks see one task either way. A string that opens as an object or a list but does not decode is handed on as it is, and refused as `brief is not valid JSON`: the model can mend broken JSON, and cannot send the part as anything but a string. Any other string stays a string, and the structure check refuses it in its own words, `brief must be a JSON object`. The descriptions of the three fields say that each is a JSON object. — *Why:* the live run of T53.2 found Claude sending all three parts as strings of JSON, as a client sends a field whose schema names no type, and the model cannot choose otherwise. Its first attempt was refused `bad_structure` with no check run, and it rightly declined to spend a second in the same form (`docs/live/05-login-drive.md`, finding 4). The author chose to fix it at once. — *Rejected:* typing the three fields as objects in the input schema, as the model's own reply in the run proposed: a client that still sent a string would then be refused by the library, whose message quotes the value — the whole task with its answer, in a tool that draws a card (remark 36); asking the model to send objects, which it cannot. — *Where it lives:* `internal/transport/mcp/submittask.go`, `task_test.go` and `fuzz_test.go`; SPEC 7.2; `docs/live/05-login-drive.md`; RUN.md (T53.2).

**R101. The tool of the answer: what it takes, what it says, and when it writes.** *(Only an answer recorded here is told again: R126 seals the task anew with the letter it was given.)* `submit_answer` takes the task's id, the answer and `hint_used`, which is false when left out. The answer is read as a child or a model types it — a letter in either case, with the spaces around it dropped, or `?` for "I don't know" — and anything else is refused by the tool, `status: rejected` with `code: invalid_arguments`, naming the rule and never the value. The profile judges the answer, not the tool: the sealed part of the task says whether a letter is right and which trap stands behind a wrong one, and `?` is a wrong answer that chose no option and took no trap (R93), so its entry in the window is `confused`, never right, with no `chosen` and no `trap` — and the profile and its schema refuse one that says otherwise. The answer is recorded once. The task stays on the card with the answer it was given — the choice, the hint, the level in the topic before and after, and which answer of the trial series it was, as `current_task.answered` — until the next task is asked for; the same task answered again, from a second tab, after a reply lost on its way, or by the model after the card, opens the seal again and is told the same result word for word with `already_answered`, and nothing is written and no line is left. `next_task` takes an answered task off the card with nothing recorded — it is no skip — and a task handed out over one replaces it the same way. An answer to a task that is not on the card is `status: stale`, `code: stale_task`, on the `task` screen: the card that sent it stays on its task, and the words say what is on the card now without repeating an id the profile does not know — and, for a task whose answer the window keeps, that the answer is recorded, lest the model tell the child it was lost. A task whose seal can no longer be opened is taken off the card, the profile written, and the model told in a failure of ours with a sentence of its own, error `sealed`: no answer is recorded, and no skip, since the child did not leaf past it; a task that had its answer before the seal was lost is told in a second sentence that the answer counts and only telling it again is gone. The result is the same in both modes — `screen: result`, and `result` with the choice, whether it was right, the right option, the trap as `{id, text}`, the solution, the hint and the rating in the topic before and after, or how far the trial series has got — and the words tell the model how to explain it, around the task's own texts, quoted. `answer_recorded` is written once the file holds the answer and carries the version of the instructions the task was written to; the topic, the trap and that version come from a file a person can edit, so the line holds the first two to the catalog and the version to the shape of the service's own, `other` otherwise; the span `record_answer`, under the call, says whether the answer was right and names the trap by its catalog id, and nothing else — no letter, no pace, no rating. A write that lost to another is the failure of R99 and is not retried here: the same call made again finds the answer recorded, or records it. — *Why:* 03-flows asks for exactly this — the first call records, any later one returns the same recorded result and writes nothing — and the right option, the trap and the solution live only in the seal, which used to leave the file with the task, so nothing could tell the result again; a card whose reply was lost, or a second tab, has to be able to draw its result. The author chose the kept answer over a shorter telling. The answered task leaves at the next ask rather than at the next hand-out, so that a lesson never holds an answered task beside an open request. Judging in the profile makes an entry that is both "I don't know" and right impossible to build rather than merely refused. — *Rejected:* telling a repeated answer from the window — right or wrong and the letter, with no right option and no solution — which leaves a card that lost its reply with no result to draw; refusing it as the prototype did, which breaks 03-flows and the tool's idempotence; keeping the answered task until the next one is handed out; the answer held to a list in the input schema, whose library quotes back what broke it; a measurement of answers, which the line already counts; recording a task whose seal is lost as skipped. — *Consequence:* the answered task keeps its open part and its seal in the file until the next ask, no larger than while it was being worked on. `current_task.answered` is optional and did not raise the version: until T50 no file is shared between builds, and T50 decides whether what an older build would make of it — an answered task recorded as skipped as well — is a version and a migration or a window written down (SPEC remark 44). The instructions tell the model to record "I don't know" as `?` and that recording twice returns what was recorded. — *Where it lives:* `internal/domain/profile/answer.go`, `task.go`, `request.go`, `validate.go` and `profile.schema.json`; `internal/transport/mcp/submitanswer.go`, `submittask.go`, `events.go` and `failure.go`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; SPEC 5.9, 7.2, 7.3, 7.4, 12.2, 12.5 and remarks 36, 43 and 44; `docs/architecture/02-auth.md`, `03-flows.md` and `04-profile.md`; RUN.md (T45).

**R102. `next_task` answers in words alone.** *(Since T62.8 `next_task` draws a card, with a payload and an output schema; the words alone, and the package in them, are `get_package`'s: R152.)* Its result has no payload, and the tool declares no output schema: a short paragraph for the model — which request is open, in which language, that the task is the model's to write and is handed in with its id, and the last recorded answer — and then the package. A request already open comes back the same way, saying since when; its words ask for the task if the model has written it, and only then offer the package to a model that has not — a turn cut short, say. They no longer say the task is being written: to a model that has not written one, that reads as the service at work. Two chats writing for one request at once remain possible, and share its three attempts until one task is accepted; after that the other is stale and spends nothing. A refusal is the one exception: it keeps its small payload, `screen`, `last_answer`, `status`, `code` and `problems`, because its status is what marks it a refusal, for the model and in the log. — *Why:* the local run in Claude Code 2.1.280 found that a host may show the model a result's payload in place of its words. The model received `{"request_id": …, "screen": "waiting"}` and never the package, took the task for something the service was preparing, and asked for it fifteen times in thirty seconds without writing one. The words were the only place the package travelled (R100), and the payload of `next_task` has no reader but the model, since no card is drawn from it. The author chose words alone over the package in the payload. — *Rejected:* the package in the payload, with the words the same JSON — safe, since `next_task` draws no card, but it undoes the rule that no payload ever holds the package, which is the simplest way to be sure no card ever shows one; the words of every tool carried in its payload as well — the sturdiest against such hosts and the largest change, left until T63 shows whether ChatGPT needs it (SPEC remark 45). — *Consequence:* `next_task` is the one tool without an output schema (SPEC 7.1); `request_id`, `already_open` and `age_seconds` leave the payload, and the words say them, the last answer among them. The frame still reads a refusal's status from its payload. The other tools' guidance words do not reach a model in such a host: it acts on the payload, the server's instructions and the tools' descriptions — as much of each as the host keeps (R103). — *Where it lives:* `internal/transport/mcp/nexttask.go`; SPEC 7.1, 7.3 and remarks 16 and 45; `docs/architecture/03-flows.md`; `docs/live/04-local-run.md`; RUN.md (T46).

**R103. The rules of a lesson come first in the instructions, within 2,048 characters.** *(Since T62.2 "Always" also says that a task a card shows gets no word of the model's own until the child answers or asks, and the words that come with the task — the acceptance and a task handed in again — say it where it applies; `next_task` tells the model that its package and what it says of past answers are for it alone; an answer's words ask for praise of the step, not of the child; and `get_profile` is no longer the call a lesson starts with: a task needs only `next_task`, which says when there is no profile. The first live run in Claude found the model explaining under the card why the task came and how to solve it, and saying the child "solved" it in the feminine.)* *(Since T64.5 the same words — the acceptance and a task handed in again, one text for both — say that where the card shows the task the child answers there, and the card records the answer itself, so the model asks for no answer in the chat, and that without a card the model reads the task out and records the answer given in the chat; the descriptions of `submit_task` and `submit_answer` say it of a host that shows cards, and the lesson's steps say the child answers on the card. The acceptance ended with "record the answer with submit_answer", and in T64.4 a model on Claude's free plan asked the parent under the card to type the letter in, a message of the free plan's for each task. "Always" says it already, "An answer on a card is recorded without you", and stays as it is: it ends 10 characters short of 2,048.)* The server's instructions open with a section of their own, "Always", that says what must hold in a lesson whatever the host: a pseudonym only, and never in a task; the language of the chat, and wording that does not show whether the child is a boy or a girl; the answer, the solution and the explanations kept until the child has answered, and the hint given only when asked; the answer recorded before anything is explained, "I don't know" as `?`; a wrong answer explained from its trap's text, with the right option at the end of the solution; the next task asked for only when the child wants it; and `last_answer` read before the current task is spoken of. The instructions up to the end of that section are at most 2,048 characters long, and the sections after it say how. Every tool's description is at most 2,048 characters as well, so `next_task` lists the topics by id with the levels each is taught at, as SPEC 7.2 has it, and no longer with what each topic is. — *Why:* the local run in Claude Code 2.1.280 found that a host may cut them: it keeps the first 2,048 characters of a server's instructions and of each tool's description, and marks the cut "… [truncated]" (SPEC remark 46). The instructions were 5,298 characters long, and the model was given the child, the profile and the start of how a task is asked for, and nothing about the answer or the next task: it asked for the next task after every answer on its own, and explained a wrong answer from the right option as often as from its trap. The words of `submit_answer`, which say the same, do not reach a model in that host either (R102). The description of `next_task` was 2,780 characters long and lost the topics at the end of its list. With the rules first, the model stopped after every explanation and waited to be asked, and began the one wrong answer of the run from its trap. — *Rejected:* raising the limit for the local runs, which tests a host nobody has; the whole instructions cut down to 2,048 characters, which takes from the hosts that read them whole what only a host that cuts would lose; the rules carried in the payload of every result, the larger change R102 left until T63 shows whether it is needed. — *Consequence:* each rule is said once, in "Always", and the sections below say how a lesson goes; a model chooses a topic of its own by the id alone, and the package describes the topic it writes on. A test of the content holds "Always" to be the first section and to end within the length, and the test of the tool listing holds every description to it. — *Where it lives:* `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md` and `content/content_test.go`; `internal/transport/mcp/nexttask.go`, `profile_test.go` and `task_test.go`; SPEC 7.1 and remarks 28, 45 and 46; `docs/live/04-local-run.md`; RUN.md (T46).

**R129. A card speaks the language the parent chose for the cards, then the host's, then English, each found by its full tag, a shorter one or the script it implies.** *(Since T81 the ranks have no names, in the dictionaries or anywhere else: R213.)* *(Since T62.3 a task card speaks its task's language and a waiting card its request's, R144.)* The widget's words are `web/locales/<tag>.json`, all of them inlined into the page. `en` and `ru` start as the draft's: the strings of `DEFAULT_STRINGS` but the demo's three, the five steps of the waiting screen and the grade's two labels, the grade a slot, `{grade}` — 45 keys in dot notation, the first word the screen or the speaker. A card's language is chosen from a list: `ui_language` as its payload carries it — in `child` on the task and waiting cards, which `submit_task` now fills, and in `profile` on the progress and the profile — then the host's `locale`, which the bridge reads from the app context and follows when it changes. Each is read with `Intl.Locale` and tried whole, then shorter, then with the script its language is usually written in — `pt-BR`, `pt`; `zh-CN`, `zh-Hans` — but only under a tag written in that script: `zh-TW` never finds a bare `zh`, which stands for the simplified characters, nor `pa-Arab` a bare `pa`, written in Gurmukhi. A tag that names no language, `und` or `und-RU`, is not tried at all. The first dictionary found is spoken, English when there is none, and the document gets its tag as `lang` and, from its script, its `dir`. The words are read by `text(key, slots)`: a number in a slot is written as the language writes numbers, `{count}` chooses a plural form, and a key the dictionary lacks is taken from its shorter tag in its script and then from English, counted by that language's rules. A development build — the widget's tests among them — throws instead, and so it does for a slot left empty and a plural wording given no count. The keys are typed from `en.json`; a Vitest test holds every dictionary to English's keys and slots and to braces that are placeholders alone, each plural wording to its language's categories and each file to its canonical tag, and shows on broken words that it catches each. — *Why:* SPEC 7.5 lets the parent's choice override the host's, and the task card, drawn by `submit_task`, could not know it: its `child` carried the pseudonym and the grade alone. A choice the widget has no words for falls to the host's language rather than to English, because the parent's pick says nothing for English over what the host reads. The script step lets `zh-Hans`, one of SPEC 8.7's languages, be found from the `zh-CN` a host sends, so the dictionaries of T59 are files and no code; a direction read from the script does the same for `ar`, `fa` and `ur`. The key test runs in Vitest rather than in Go, where RUN.md placed it before the widget had a test runner (R84): the dictionaries belong to the widget, their plural categories are `Intl.PluralRules`'s, and English's keys are the widget's types. — *Rejected:* the parent's choice replacing the host's outright, which sends a language the widget lacks to English; a list of right-to-left languages in code; the widget's words in a Go package, or read by a Go test from `web/`, only to be checked in Go; a missing key said as the key in every build, which hides a gap until a child sees it. — *Consequence:* `child` in `submit_task` gains `ui_language`, and its output schema with it. The progress screen's rating has an accessible name in the draft, "Overall rating", that is neither in `DEFAULT_STRINGS` nor on the Russian page; it joins the dictionary when the progress is ported (T57). So do the names of the eleven ranks, which R12 and О-48 put among these keys: neither the draft nor any document words them, and they reach the dictionary with the screen that shows them, where the author reads them. — *Where it lives:* `web/locales/`, `web/src/i18n/`, `web/src/widget/words.ts`, `bridge.ts` and `WidgetApp.tsx`; `internal/transport/mcp/submittask.go`; SPEC 7.3, 7.5, 8.6, 8.8 and 8.12; RUN.md (T54, T55–T57, T59).

**R144. The language the parent chose in the profile is the lesson's: the task is written in it, its card speaks it, and the model talks in it.** *(Decided by the author in T62, 2026-10-02, the model's own words among it; carried out in T62.3.)* *(Since T64.5 a card of a task asked for speaks, before the result, the language the call was made with rather than the host's, and a card of `next_task` whose payload names none speaks it too: R190.)* When the profile names a language, `ui_language`, the request `next_task` opens records it as the task's language, whatever chat language the model passes; when the profile names none, the request records the chat's, as before (`profile.Student.LessonLanguage`). `next_task` still takes the chat's language and still requires it, so that a profile with no language never leads to a guess; asked again, it compares the lesson's language rather than the chat's, so the same ask in the chat's language finds nothing to set aside. A card of a task, or of a wait for one — drawn by a refusal or a stale request —, speaks the lesson's language, which `submit_task` carries in a field of its own, `language`: the task's, or the awaited request's while it is still open; `child.ui_language` keeps saying what the parent chose, and a card drawn before the field existed reads it, as it did. A language typed into the file by hand that names no language counts as no choice (`profile.Student.ChosenLanguage`), since it would otherwise become the language a task is written and checked in. So the words on the buttons and the wording of the task are always in one language, the parent's choice or the chat's. The model talks in it too: "Always" says to talk in the profile's language, or else the chat's, `next_task`'s description says the task comes in it, and the words of `next_task` name the language the parent chose, since a lesson starts there and the model may not have read the profile. A task already asked for keeps the language it was asked in when the parent chooses another, and the words of the profile and of a request asked for again say so: the language chosen starts with the next task. The profile and the progress keep R129's order — the profile's language, then the host's, then English — and a card drawn before its result speaks the host's, since it has no payload yet. The profile's field keeps its name in the file, `ui_language`, and is labelled "Language of the lessons" on the card. — *Why:* in the live run a language changed in the profile changed the buttons and left the task in the chat's language, one card in two languages. The author's decision: the words on the buttons mean nothing without the task's, so the two always change together, and the model talks in the same language, since a mistake is explained from its trap's text, which is in the task's language. — *Rejected:* the profile's language for the cards alone, as R100 and R129 had it; a task card speaking its task's language while the profile's language stays the cards' alone, which would leave a language changed in the profile unable to reach the task; leaving it as it was, with the model explaining that the tasks follow the chat; the model talking in the chat's language around a task in another, a lesson in two languages; the request's language put into `child.ui_language`, one field saying two things; `task.language` read first, which would turn an old task card in an old chat to another language under the child's eyes; renaming the field in the file, a migration for a word. — *Where it lives:* `internal/domain/profile/student.go`; `internal/transport/mcp/nexttask.go`, `submittask.go`, `profile.go` and `payload.go`; `web/src/widget/words.ts` and the `profile.language` words in `web/locales/`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; SPEC 1.5, 4.3, 7.2, 7.3 and 7.5; О-14 and PRODUCT-V1 4.6 and 11; CLAUDE.md; [the T62 report](live/07-acceptance-claude.md), finding 10.

**R145. A task card has no question field and does not wait for the next task; the waiting screen has no warm-up.** *(Since T78.2 the row ends, after the trial series, with the topic's button, and a topic chosen asks for the next task as "Another task" does: R194.)* *(Since T62.8 the new card below is the one `next_task` draws as the task is asked for, and it turns into the task: R152.)* *(The card a refused task draws is R146's since T62.5: no steps, no clock.)* *(Decided by the author in T62, 2026-10-02, after the first live run in Claude; carried out in T62.4.)* The task card has no field to type a question into: the child asks in the chat. "Another task" still sends its label to the chat as the child's message (R131), but the card keeps its task and says that, once the ask reaches the chat, the new one will come below, in a card of its own — no generating steps, no clock and no deadline on a card that cannot see whether its ask was sent. While the ask is on its way the button takes no second press; once the host has it, the button may be pressed again, since a host may hold the message for the person to send, and a second ask while a task is being written is handed the request already open. A refusal by the host is said on the card. The waiting screen a refused task draws has no warm-up. A card the model's last attempt was refused on says the task did not work out and that a new one will come below — the model is told to ask for one at once —, with no steps and nothing to press, since it cannot see the new one being written. — *Why:* in the live run Claude put every message a card sent into the chat's input under a warning, for the person to send. A question typed into the card's field reached nobody by itself, and its note "Sent to the chat" was untrue; the card that turned to the wait after "Another task" ticked off steps for two minutes while the ask sat unsent. The author found the warm-up of no use, and decided that a field that cannot reach the model goes. — *Rejected:* keeping the field with a note that the words wait in the chat's input, which makes it the chat's own field again — Claude's guidelines advise against an app field that repeats the chat's; the old card's wait kept and cut short, which still claims work it cannot see; the button removed, which the author keeps; "Ask again" on the card whose task did not work out, a second message to the same warning. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/widget/TaskCard.tsx`, `LessonFoot.tsx`, `lesson.ts`, `WaitingCard.tsx`, `waiting.ts` and `ChatRequest.tsx`; the dictionaries in `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`; SPEC 8.2, 8.3 and 8.12; PRODUCT-V1 4.4 and О-26; the instructions for the model; [the T62 report](live/07-acceptance-claude.md), findings 5, 6, 8 and 9.

**R146. A card a task is being handed in to waits for it by what the host tells of the call, and a card a task did not come to says so without a clock.** *(Superseded by R152 since T62.8: the card is drawn by `next_task` and asks the service how its task stands, and a card drawn for `submit_task` draws nothing before its result.)* *(Decided by the author in T62, 2026-10-02; carried out in T62.5.)* The widget's bridge keeps one fact besides the result: the call that drew the card — the tool, by the name the host's `toolInfo` gives it, matched by its end since each host writes its own prefix before it (R07), and how far the call has got: started, running once its arguments have come whole, or cancelled. It keeps nothing of the arguments, and listens for no partial ones, which the library then drops: a task handed in carries its answer in them. Before its result, a card drawn for `submit_task` shows, after 400 ms — so that a result that comes with the call does not make a wait flash past — the steps of a task taken up as the call moves: the topic picked, the task written while its arguments come, checked once they have come whole; the result then takes the wait's place in the same card. A call cancelled says the task was not finished, and no result two minutes after the call last moved on says no task is being prepared; both say a task can be asked for in the chat, and nothing on the card asks. A card with no `toolInfo`, or drawn for another tool, draws nothing before its result, as before. A card a task did not come to — a try that failed its checks, a request that is over, the last try refused — says what happened and that the next one comes below or, if none does, that a task can be asked for in the chat, with no clock and nothing to press; the day's limit stays as R134 drew it. — *Why:* in the live run a task asked for in the chat came with no wait at all, the card's frame empty until the result, and the wait a refusal drew ticked off steps by the clock for two minutes over work it could not see, then offered "Ask again", a message Claude held under a warning. The author asked for a new card with the wait first and the task after it. The host tells a card when the arguments have come and when the call is cancelled (MCP Apps), so the steps can follow the call rather than a clock; the step of readability, which needs the grade, goes, since no payload has come yet. — *Rejected:* steps by the clock (R92, R134); a resource per tool, so that a card knows its tool from its page, which breaks the one resource of SPEC 8.1 and serves the page twice, while `toolInfo` was seen in Claude; a card for `next_task` that polls for the task, a Drive read per poll and a second card; "Ask again" on a card a task did not come to; the partial arguments read to show progress, which would bring the answer into the page. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/widget/bridge.ts`, `ComingCard.tsx`, `WidgetApp.tsx`, `WaitingCard.tsx`, `waiting.ts` and `payload.ts`; the dictionaries in `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/` and `web/scripts/layout.ts`; SPEC 8.2, 8.4, 8.11, 8.12 and remarks 61 and 64; `docs/architecture/03-flows.md`; PRODUCT-V1 4.4 and О-26; [the T62 report](live/07-acceptance-claude.md), findings 4 and 6.

**R152. The card of a task is drawn as the task is asked for, waits for it by asking the service, and turns into it; the package reaches the model through a tool of its own.** *(Decided by the author in T62.8, 2026-10-03.)* *(R217: a request handed back no longer says since when.)* *(Since R201 a card that waited for its task ticks off the checks and the task ready once the task is on the card, and turns into it under a second later.)*
- **`next_task` draws the card** (`ui.resourceUri`), with a payload of its own and an output schema.
  - A request opened or handed back is `screen: coming`: its id, whether it was open already and since when, whom the card is for, the lesson's language and the last answer.
  - A refusal is `screen: waiting` with its status, and now the child too; with no profile it is `first_run`.
  - Its words carry no package. They say the request is open and send the model to `get_package` at once, then to `submit_task`. The description says the same, for a host that shows the model the payload in place of the words.
  - It still builds the package before it writes a request, only to be sure one can be built.
- **`get_package`** is for the model and draws no card. It takes the request's id.
  - For the request open and awaited it answers in words alone, with the package: the rule of R102 is now this tool's.
  - Any other request is `stale`, `stale_request`, with a small payload, and words that send the model to the request open when there is one; with no profile, the first sign-in's words.
- **`read_task`** is for the card alone (`ui.visibility: ["app"]`). It takes the request's id, reads the profile and writes nothing. It answers with one of:
  - the task being written, with how many attempts the checks turned down (`screen: coming`, `refused`);
  - the request's task on the card, answered or not, as `submit_task` hands a card a task (`screen: task`);
  - none coming (`screen: waiting`, `code: stale_request`), or the first sign-in.

  It never carries a status, so its line counts an answer.
- **A task's id is its request's**, `tsk_` for `req_` (`profile.TaskIDFor`), so the file need not say which request a task was written for. `profile.TaskFor` says how the task of a request stands, and the transport draws the payload from it.
- **`submit_task` draws no card.**
  - Its payload keeps the shape a card is drawn from, for a host that still draws one from an earlier list of the tools and for a card of an earlier chat drawn again.
  - Its words say the card `next_task` drew turns into the task, and that without a card, or if the child says the card shows no task, the model reads it out.
- **Before `next_task`'s result**, a card the host's `toolInfo` names it for shows, after 400 ms, the topic being picked.
  - A cancelled call says the task was not finished.
  - No answer after two minutes says the task is taking longer than usual.
- **With the result, the card asks `read_task`.**
  - When it asks:
    - first after a moment of its request's own, under 1.5 s, so that the cards of a chat drawn again do not all ask at once;
    - then every 4 s while the task is written;
    - every 15 s, and the request's own moment more, once two minutes have passed with no news (the card drawn, the task heard of, a try turned down), or after a question that went unanswered, so that cards held back together do not ask again together;
    - one question at a time, none while the page is out of sight, and one at once when it is looked at again;
    - no more once twenty questions in a row have gone unanswered: five minutes at the slow pace, past any wait a task takes, the card still saying the task is taking long.
  - What it shows:
    - the topic picked, the task being written, and a try turned down with a new one being written;
    - the task, once on the card, in the wait's place in the same frame, the progress opened over it left open;
    - a request that is over, at the first answer that says so, settled at the second, since a read straight after a write may bring the file as it was before it.
    - The words of a request that is over are the same whatever ended it — no task is here, and where the next one comes —, since the card cannot tell the tries spent from a request replaced, or from a task answered elsewhere while the page was out of sight.
  - A result with no payload and no failure draws nothing.
- **The paces.**
  - The account's pace goes to sixty a minute, burst twenty: a card asks fifteen times a minute while a task is written, beside the model's calls, and a chat opened again asks once or twice for every card it draws.
  - The instance's two hundred stays, since the same number holds the door of the sign-in: about a dozen children waiting for a task in the same minute on one instance. T64 measures it, and parts the two doors' numbers if need be.

— *Why:* in the author's check in Claude on 2026-10-03, the card a task is waited in came about a minute after the ask.
- It was drawn by `submit_task`, which Claude calls once the model has written the task in its thinking.
- `next_task` drew none, though Claude calls it at once and draws a card's frame as its call starts ([the T62 report](live/07-acceptance-claude.md), finding 4).
- That is the new evidence against the rejections of R134 and R146, which counted on a wait that starts with the hand-in.
- The card `next_task` draws receives the whole of its result, so the package cannot travel in it, and goes to a tool that draws nothing.
- A card cannot learn of a call that did not draw it (MCP Apps), so it asks; a Drive read for each question is the price the author accepted.
- The author chose that a card drawn again with a chat says its task is no longer here, rather than keeping past tasks' texts in the profile.

— *Rejected:*
- the package with `next_task` and a card drawn by a tool the model calls after it, which leaves a model that forgets the call with no card at all;
- the package in `next_task`'s result, which the card would receive;
- a field of the payload telling the model to call `get_package`, R102's words in a payload for one tool: the description and the instructions carry it;
- versioning the widget's address now (SPEC remark 38), left to what the live run sees;
- a question the service holds open until there is news, which a host may cut and an instance pays for while it waits;
- the texts of the latest tasks kept in the profile, for old cards;
- "This task didn't work out" on a card that saw its task being written and then heard it was not coming, which is untrue of a task answered on another device and replaced while the page was out of sight;
- the task's request named in a field of the file, which an older build drops when it writes;
- raising the instance's pace with the account's, which would raise the door of the sign-in.

— *Consequence:*
- A task costs one Drive read more for its package, and one for each question a card asks: about fifteen for a minute's wait.
- With CPU allocated only while a request is served, that is about four seconds of an instance's time.
- Each question leaves a `tool_call` and a `drive_call` line.
- A chat begun before the change has no `get_package`, and its model is told to have the adult start a new one.
- A page kept from the release before draws the new card as one it cannot show, and the child reads the task when the model reads it out.

— *Where it lives:*
- `internal/domain/profile/request.go`;
- `internal/transport/mcp/nexttask.go`, `getpackage.go`, `readtask.go`, `submittask.go`, `payload.go`, `service.go`;
- `internal/config`;
- `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`;
- `web/src/widget/ComingCard.tsx`, `ChoosingCard.tsx`, `TaskWait.tsx`, `coming.ts`, `waiting.ts`, `payload.ts`, `TaskCard.tsx`, `WidgetApp.tsx` and `bridge.ts`;
- the dictionaries in `web/locales/`;
- `web/src/preview/`;
- `tools/load/lesson` and `tools/load/scenario`;
- SPEC 7.1–7.3, 8.2–8.4, 8.11, 10.2, 11.2 and remarks 16, 38, 61, 64 and 66;
- `docs/architecture/03-flows.md`, `04-profile.md` and `05-storage.md`;
- PRODUCT-V1 4.4 and О-26.

**R190. The card of a task asked for speaks, before the result, the language the call was made with, and says nothing until the host has told it.** *(Decided in T64.5, 2026-10-04.)* A host hands a card the arguments of the call it was drawn for, whole, before the result (`ui/notifications/tool-input`, the library's `toolinput`), and `next_task` is always called with `language`. The bridge keeps that argument and nothing else of the arguments, and only once they come whole and before the result: told after it, they would turn a card already drawn to another language. The card of a task asked for speaks it until the result comes, and the card the result draws speaks it too when its payload names no language — a refusal of the ask, the first sign-in. Until the host has told the arguments the card shows nothing; a call cancelled before then is said in the host's language, since nothing comes after it to speak another. — *Why:* in T64.4 the waiting card spoke the host's language, English, the language of the author's Claude, and turned to the lesson's, Russian, as the result came: a child saw the wait begin in one language and go on in another. The chat's language is the lesson's unless the parent chose another, and the model passes it with every ask. — *Rejected:* the host's language before the result, as R144 had it, above; a card blank before every result, which leaves the first seconds of an ask empty on a host that tells the arguments; the arguments still coming, whose language may be cut short, `r` for `ru`; the arguments kept whole, which carry the answer of a task handed in from an earlier list of the tools. — *Consequence:* when the parent chose a language other than the chat's, a wait asked in the chat's turns to the parent's as the result comes, in R144's order, since the ask cannot know the profile. A host that never tells the arguments gets no wait before the result, as a rule a moment, nor the word that the task is taking long when the result is two minutes late. The language is read only on the card of a task's ask: what the arguments of another call name says nothing of the lesson. The library holds a host to telling them once, after the handshake and before the result; whether Claude does is for the live run of T64.5 to see. The preview's host tells a task caught being asked for the language the page is shown in. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/widget/bridge.ts`, `WidgetApp.tsx`, `ChoosingCard.tsx` and their tests, `rtl.test.tsx`; `web/src/preview/HostedCard.tsx`; SPEC 7.5, 8.2 and 8.4; [the T64.4 report](live/09-load.md), finding 2.

**R147. The progress is one screen after the author's mockup: each topic has a rank of its own beside the overall one, every rank drawn as a course filled as far as its rating has come; the topics within reach are listed before they are met; the skipped tasks are counted once, in all; and the progress says where the profile's file is.** *(Since T81 the overall rank and each topic's are told by their numbers, "Rank 3", and the overall course marks the grades each run of ranks roughly matches: R213.)* *(Since T74.3 a topic even with the overall rank says so — "even" — as the mockup 4a has it, and the moves are drawn on the courses: R171. Its rank's name in plain text stands.)* *(Since T74.1 the screen folds its topics, mistakes, latest answers and profile into sections under the rank and "Next up", and the overall rank has no rating's number beside it: R162.)* *(Since T62.7 a search for the file that fails shows the progress without "Your data", rather than failing it, and "Edit" opens the form: R148.)* *(Decided by the author in T62, 2026-10-02, and in the planning of T62.6, 2026-10-03; carried out in T62.6. Supersedes R95.)* The screen heads with the overall rank's name, large, over "rank 3 of 11 · rating 1573" — the rating's number is shown at the overall rank alone — and a course of the eleven ranks: those below filled, the one reached filled as far as the rating has come through it, and the next line "Next rank — Hill. Right answers move the bar forward, mistakes a little back", or "This is the highest rank" at the top. How far through its rank a rating has come, the share, is a whole percent of the way from the rank's floor to the next one's, on the rating as drawn and rounded down, so that the next rank is never drawn as reached before it is; the first rank, which has no floor, is counted from a step below the second's, 1168, and the last, which has no next one, is drawn with the whole way behind it. "Next up" stays as it was. The topics are those met — answered or skipped a task of — and those the rule could set now, its own list of the topics within reach, in catalog order, so that the list grows with the child. Each has its rank's name, a word when that rank is above or below the overall one — "ahead" or "behind", compared rank for rank so that the word agrees with the names drawn, and none for the overall rank itself — the mark of a mastered topic, and a course of the eleven ranks filled as its own share says, in one of five steps of a ramp by its rank: ranks 1–3 orange, 4–5 amber, 6–7 yellow, 8–9 lime, 10–11 green, and never the red of a wrong answer. A topic with no answer yet — never given, on the card now, or only skipped — has its course drawn open and "no answers yet". During the trial series its place is taken by "Trial series", "3 of 5", a course of five steps and "Ranks come after 5 tasks", and every topic is drawn open with "no rank yet". The five latest entries stay, and under them how many tasks were left without an answer in all, one number the service counts — every topic's count added up, one the catalog no longer has too — and the words for the model say the same number and name the same five entries, a skip among them as skipped. A mistake that repeats has a dot for each time, up to five, beside how many times, in a frame of its own. "Profile · for the parent" is a frame with "Edit profile" at its head — its words still go to the chat until T62.7 — and the interests and the skills as names each apart. "Your data" is a frame under it in the same style, each part under the question it answers — where the profile is, how to delete it, how to cut off access, how to remove the app — rather than a verb that reads as a button; `get_progress` and `read_progress` carry `location` as `get_profile` does, so that the progress, the one card a parent sees the profile on once T62.7 takes `get_profile`'s away, draws it. The payload adds `overall.share`, each topic's `rank`, `share` and `compared`, null together with its rating, the total `skipped`, and `location`, which a store in memory leaves out; a topic's `rating` stays, since a host's cached page of an earlier widget reads a progress only with it. — *Why:* finding 12 of the T62 report — the author's mockup `rework/3a-ranks.png`, a style that all the screen's content fits into, "Your data" included — and finding 3 of the T53.2 report, the headings of "Your data" read as buttons that did nothing; and the author's answers of 2026-10-03: the topics met and within reach, the trial series in the rank's place with the topics drawn open, the rating's number at the overall rank alone, and a ramp with no red, since the ladder is one for grades 1 to 6 and a child of grade 1 starts in the third rank. R95 rejected a rank on each topic's row as crowding a row at 320 px; the layout check, every language at 320, 360 and 640 px in two engines, shows the row fits, with the word and the rank's name taking a line of their own where they must. — *Rejected:* the topics met alone, which the author decided against; the whole catalog, seventeen topics, most of a younger child's list empty for years; each topic's number beside its rank, where the course already shows where the topic stands in its rank, and the number stays in the words for the model; comparing a topic by the sign of its correction, which could say "ahead" beside the overall rank's own name; a word for an even rank; the skips counted over the history window, where the card has always said "in all"; the mockup's ramp from red, which a child of grade 1 would see for months in the colour of a wrong answer; the rank's name in its colour, where yellow text on white falls short of the contrast text needs; `location` on `get_progress` alone, where R97 holds the two tools of the progress to one payload; and the folder's name remembered to spare the calls, where R120 remembers only the revision. — *Consequence:* opening the progress costs Drive two calls more, the search and the folder's name, as `get_profile` does (R120), and a search that fails fails the progress; the time it takes over a task is measured in the re-acceptance of T62. A progress card of an earlier chat still draws (SPEC remark 38): with no share the rank's step is drawn empty, a topic with no rank has an empty course and no word, a progress with no total adds its topics' counts, and one with no location shows no parent's data. Keys are added and removed in all 22 dictionaries, and five tokens, `--ramp-1` to `--ramp-5`, in both themes, held by a test to 3 to 1 against the card and to 8° on the colour wheel from one step to the next. The screen departs from the approved draft (R89, SPEC 8.12) and follows the mockup. — *Where it lives:* `internal/domain/rating/scale.go`, `internal/domain/tutor/tutor.go` (`WithinReach`), `internal/domain/progress/progress.go`, `internal/transport/mcp/progress.go` and `payload.go`; `internal/widget/tokens.css`; `web/src/design/progress.tsx` and `mathtrail.css`; `web/src/widget/ProgressScreen.tsx`, `ProfileScreen.tsx`, `payload.ts` and `names.ts`; the dictionaries in `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; SPEC 2.4, 7.1, 7.3, 8.2, 8.11, 8.12 and remark 38; PRODUCT-V1 4.2, 4.5 and scenario 5; `docs/architecture/04-profile.md` and `05-storage.md`; [the T62 report](live/07-acceptance-claude.md), finding 12, and [the T53.2 report](live/05-login-drive.md), finding 3.

**R148. A profile is made in the chat and changed on the card's form; neither tool of the profile draws a card.** *(Since T78.2 a task's card writes the topic of the lessons with `edit_profile` too, a third thing a card writes: R194.)* *(Decided by the author in T62, 2026-10-02, and in the planning of T62.7, 2026-10-03; carried out in T62.7. Supersedes what R91, R97, R99 and R135 say of the profile's card, its first sign-in and "Edit profile".)* `get_profile` and `save_profile` answer in words and with the payload they had, and name no widget: the adult's screen no longer opens in a lesson in front of the child, and the model says in a sentence what was saved. A new adult is led through the profile by the words of whichever tool finds none, in the chat: they say first that they are the child's parent or tutor, then give a pseudonym — never a name, an age or a school — and the grade. The first sign-in's card, drawn where a tool with a card finds no profile, only tells them so, with a fourth note, "How to start", and has neither the tick nor the button. "Edit" at the head of "Profile · for the parent" opens a form in the frame's place: the pseudonym, the grade, the interests as chips added and taken out, the twenty-five skills as ticks, and the language of the lessons — the twenty-two the widget speaks, by name and in the card's order of names, after "The chat's language", and one the chat chose outside them as it is kept. The parent's notes are not on it: they are said to the model. The form holds the details to the profile's own limits, which a test holds to its schema, and sends only what changed — a pseudonym compared without the spaces around it, the interests in their order, the skills as a set, an interest still being typed among them — so that a change the model made in the chat while the form was open is kept. It is saved by `edit_profile`, a tool the card alone calls, as it calls `read_progress` (R97): `ui.visibility: ["app"]`, no card, idempotent. It takes those five fields and nothing else — its schema refuses `notes` and `start_over` before the tool runs — makes the change by the path `save_profile` makes one, writes nothing when nothing changed, and never makes a profile: with none it answers `status: stale`, `code: stale_profile`. While a save is on its way, the form takes no second one and its fields are locked, as its buttons are. A change saved closes the form and says "Saved.", or "Saved. The new language starts with the next task." when the language changed, the card going on in the words it was drawn in (R144); the details and the header show it at once — and so do the line and the badge of the task or waiting card the progress was opened over — and the model is handed the service's words of the change, without the notes, by `updateModelContext`. A refusal opens the form again with the card's own words under each field it names; a call that failed says to try again and keeps what was typed; a profile gone says so and offers no form. Every refusal of every tool now names each problem by a code from a closed list — `required`, `too_long`, `too_many`, `entry_length`, `out_of_range`, `not_one_of`, `not_in_catalog`, `not_taught`, `not_a_language`, `empty_text`, `control_characters` — beside the rule in words, which stays for the model; a code the card has no words for is said in words that fit any. A progress or a profile whose file Drive cannot place just now — the search failed, or took too long — is shown without "Your data", with a sentence for the model, and the call that failed is the store's own line of the log; what is no failure of Drive's is told as everywhere — a profile gone between its read and that search is read again, and found gone, access taken back or ending asks for the sign-in again, and a call given up on ends. A profile's card of an earlier chat still draws, and its "Edit" opens the same form. — *Why:* findings 1 and 12 of the T62 report — the profile's card, drawn in the lesson, put the adult's screen in front of the child, and the mockup's "Edit" changes the profile in place — findings 1 and 6 of the T53.2 report — Claude holds a card's message in the chat's field under a red warning, and the model sent the parent to a card that could change nothing — and the author's answers of 2026-10-03: no tick before saving, since the adult has said who they are in the chat and the form stands under "for the parent"; no card for `save_profile` either; the progress shown without the location when the search fails; all five fields. — *Rejected:* a tick before the form is saved; a card for `save_profile`; the form calling `save_profile`, which would put the notes and starting over within a card's reach; the form sending all five fields, which writes over a change the chat made meanwhile; a field for the notes; the first profile made on the first sign-in's card; a log event of its own for a search that failed, where the store's line of the call already says it; the limits' numbers in a refusal, where the card keeps its own, held to the schema. — *Consequence:* the tools are eight. Whether Claude asks a person before a write from a card, and hides `edit_profile` from the model, is for the re-acceptance of T62 to see, as it is for an answer a card records (SPEC remark 65). A line for the model replaces the one before it, so a change on the form can replace the line of an answer; `last_answer`, in every result, makes up for it. A child may open the form as well as an adult: a risk the author accepted. Keys are added and removed in all 22 dictionaries, and "a parent or a teacher" became "a parent or a tutor" in Russian and Ukrainian, as the English and the model's words have it. — *Where it lives:* `internal/domain/profile/student.go`, `edit.go` and `answer.go`, `internal/domain/tutor/tutor.go`; `internal/transport/mcp/editprofile.go`, `profile.go`, `progress.go`, `payload.go`, `nexttask.go`, `submitanswer.go` and `service.go`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; `web/src/design/controls.tsx`, `progress.tsx` and `mathtrail.css`; `web/src/widget/editing.ts`, `ProfileForm.tsx`, `ProfileScreen.tsx`, `ProgressScreen.tsx`, `CardFrame.tsx`, `TaskCard.tsx`, `WaitingCard.tsx`, `FirstRunScreen.tsx`, `ChatRequest.tsx`, `payload.ts`, `names.ts` and `words.ts`; `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`; SPEC 5.9, 7.1, 7.2, 7.3, 8.2, 8.3, 8.11 and 8.12, remarks 16, 38 and 65; `docs/architecture/01-context.md`, `03-flows.md`, `04-profile.md` and `05-storage.md`; RUN.md (T62.7).

**R162. The progress folds its parts away under their titles, keeps them as they were left for as long as the card is drawn, and shows the overall rank without the rating's number.** *(Since T74.5 a fifth section, the review, stands between the topics and the latest answers: R180.)* *(Since 2026-10-04, at the author's request, the topics open as the screen first opens; the rest stays folded.)* *(Decided by the author in the planning of T74.1, 2026-10-03; carried out in T74.1, the first part of the progress after the author's mockup `draft/ui/profile/Рейтинг 4a.html`.)* Over the sections stand the overall rank — its name, its place out of eleven, its course and the next rank to reach — and what comes next; the rating's number is no longer drawn on the progress: the model, told so by the tool's description and its instructions, says it, and the result of an answer shows it. Under them four sections fold away under their titles, in the mockup's order: the topics; the mistakes that repeat, where the mockup's analysis will hold them; the latest answers; and the profile, "for the parent", with its fields, the form "Edit" opens and the parent's data — the profile's frame there carries no label of its own, since the section's title names it, and keeps "Edit" at its head (R148). A section with nothing in it is not drawn. Each title is a button that says whether its section is open, with a short summary by it — a dot for each of the five latest answers, in the colour of how it went, and the same in words for a screen reader alone; "for the parent" by the profile — which goes under the title where the two do not fit, and an arrow that turns down when the section is open, whichever way the card reads. As the screen first opens, the topics are open and every other section is folded. A folded section stays on the page, hidden, so that a form half filled in — what was typed, a save on its way, what became of it — is there as it was when the section opens again, and the focus stays on the title pressed. Which sections are open is kept by whoever outlives the screen: the frame of a card over a task or a wait, so that the progress opened again after "Back to task" finds them as they were, though it is read afresh; and the progress card `get_progress` draws. A new card, or a chat drawn again, starts as the screen first opens. — *Why:* the author's mockup, and the author's answers of 2026-10-03: the rating's number dropped as the mockup has it; the sections remembered while the card lives; the topics open from the start, as the author asked on 2026-10-04, since where the child stands in each is what the progress is opened for; the latest answers summed up in coloured dots as the mockup draws them, though a reader who cannot tell the colours apart sees the difference only in the section itself, where each answer has its mark and its words. — *Rejected:* a section locked while its form is edited, a button that does nothing; folded sections taken off the page, which lose a form half filled in; sections remembered across reloads, which MCP Apps gives a card no way to do (SPEC remark 64); marks in place of the dots, which the author decided against; a region role on each section's body, which the accordion pattern leaves optional and Biome refuses on a `div`; the profile's frame keeping its label under the section's title, which says the same twice, to the eye and to a screen reader. — *Consequence:* R135's progress opened again "as a screen anew" now holds for its data alone; R136's mistakes stand before the latest answers; R147's rating at the overall rank gives way. The card needs no overall rating in its payload, though the service still sends one for the model and for pages of earlier releases. Tests and scenes open a section before they look into it, since the layout check measures only what is shown: the progress over a task is measured as it first opens and with every section open, and the five scenes of the model's progress card open every section. In all 22 dictionaries `progress.rank_line` loses the rating, and `progress.for_parent` is new: each language's part of `profile.label` after its " · ". — *Where it lives:* `web/src/design/blocks.tsx` (`Fold`), `progress.tsx` (`StatusDots`, lists and frames with no label of their own) and `mathtrail.css`; `web/src/widget/folds.ts`, `ProgressScreen.tsx`, `ProfileScreen.tsx`, `CardFrame.tsx` and `payload.ts`; `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; `internal/transport/mcp/progress.go`, `profile.go` and `editprofile.go`; SPEC 7.1, 8.2, 8.11 and 8.12; `docs/architecture/03-flows.md`; `docs/parents.md`; RUN.md (T74.1).

**R165. The progress tells what moved since the last answer and over the week, each topic by its own answers, from a history the profile keeps.** *(Since T74.3 the card draws the moves, over the while a switch chooses: R171.)* *(Decided by the author in the planning of T74.2, 2026-10-03; carried out in T74.2, the second part of the progress after the author's mockup `draft/ui/profile/Рейтинг 4a.html`.)* Every answer keeps where the child stood before it moved anything — `recent[].before`, θ and the correction of the answer's topic — and the first answer after the trial series on each new date in UTC keeps where the child stood as the date began: `rating_days`, θ and the correction of every topic answered by then, oldest first, one to a date, at most seven, all at full precision. Only recording an answer writes either, before it moves the levels; reading the progress writes nothing (R119). The trial series keeps no day, and its last answer gave the first rank: a move is told from the first answer after it, and the first day starts from the levels the series ended at. A day the week ending on the answer's date no longer reaches is let go of, and an answer dated before the last day kept — the clock of another instance ran ahead — starts no day of its own. The progress tells two whiles: since the last answer — the latest answer of the window, a task left without one moving nothing — and over the week, the seven dates in UTC ending the day it is asked for. The overall rating is measured whole, from θ before the last answer or as the week began. A topic is measured by its own correction alone, from where θ stands now — from θ_now + δ_then to θ_now + δ_now — so a topic no answer of its own moved stands where it stood, though every answer moves θ under it; an answer moves θ and its topic's correction the same way, so the two are never told as moving apart. A topic first answered in the while is new. The word for a move is read off what is drawn, as R147 compares a topic's rank — `rank_up`, `forward`, `same`, `back`, `rank_down` — so a move that changes neither the rank nor the whole percent is no move. A while that cannot be told is null: during the series and at its last answer; since an answer that keeps no levels before it; over a week with an answer in it and no day kept; and over a week with an answer in it that keeps no levels before it, which a build that kept no history wrote, keeping no day, so that the first day kept is the middle of the week — the first day kept after such a write carries the latest date of an answer kept without its levels, `unkept`, so the week stays untold after that answer leaves the window of twenty, and the day the trial series ended at carries none, since the answers before it are the series'. A week with no answer in it moved nothing, and says so. The payload adds `change: {last_task, week}` beside the overall rank and each topic with one, each null or `{rating, rank, share, moved}` — a topic's earlier rating left out, since no card draws a topic's rating, and a new topic with only `moved` — and leaves `change` out where neither while can be told. The words for the model add a sentence for each while that can be told: the overall rating from and to, with its rank when it changed, and the topics whose own answers moved them, by how. The tool's description and the instructions ask the model to say a step back gently, as part of learning, never as a score against the child. The card draws none of it until T74.3. — *Why:* the mockup shows the change since the last task and over the week, and the profile kept only where the child stands now; and the author's answer of 2026-10-03 on a topic: its own shift alone, as the mockup draws the topics no answer touched, standing still. The week is in UTC because the service works the change out and is told no time zone (SPEC 8.4). — *Rejected:* the start of the week read from the file's revisions kept forever, which costs the progress two Drive calls more on every opening, a sixth method of the store past the five an interface may have, nothing at all in the store kept in memory, and a revision that holds a day's first write rather than where the day began; the levels kept in the window of twenty entries alone, which does not reach back a week; the levels worked back from the answers, which breaks the day the step of the rating changes (T72.7); a topic measured whole, θ and δ together, which moves every topic with every task in another, and which the author decided against; a week told from the first day kept whatever came before it in the week, which would pass part of a week off as the whole after a deploy or a rollback; a day kept at the trial series' last answer, which the first answer after the series keeps anyway; ranks in the history, which R12 keeps nowhere. — *Consequence:* a build before this one drops both fields when it writes, as it drops every field it does not know, so a rollback loses the history and tells less for a week — never anything false, since a while is told only when every answer in it keeps where it started. A child who answered before the deploy has no week to tell for up to seven days. The file grows by about 14 KB with thirty topics answered: a typical file is about 60 KB, and one near the caps about 80 KB, past the target of 64 KB that 04-profile set, far from the ceiling of 1 MiB. Nothing counts or shows the days the history keeps: they are dates to measure from, and the rhythm of the lessons stays unshown (R13). The privacy policy and the guide for parents say the file keeps the levels of the last seven days. The topics' earlier ratings reach the model in no words: they are named by how they moved. — *Where it lives:* `internal/domain/profile/history.go`, `answer.go`, `progress.go`, `profile.go`, `validate.go` and `profile.schema.json`; `internal/domain/progress/change.go` and `progress.go`; `internal/transport/mcp/progress.go`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; SPEC 2.10, 7.1, 7.3 and 8.4 and remark 38; `docs/architecture/04-profile.md` and `05-storage.md`; `docs/parents.md`; `site/content/en/privacy.md` and `site/content/ru/privacy.md`; PRODUCT-V1 5; RUN.md (T74.2).

**R171. The progress draws how the ranks moved over the while a switch chooses: stripes on the courses, a line under the rank, a word on each topic, the topics' count by their title, and a legend.** *(Since T81 a move goes from one rank's number to the other's: R213.)* *(Decided by the author in the planning of T74.3, 2026-10-04; carried out in T74.3, the third part of the progress after the author's mockup `draft/ui/profile/Рейтинг 4a.html`.)* Over the overall rank, once the trial series is over and the progress tells moves at all, a switch of two segments — "Last task" and "Past week" — chooses the while the moves are drawn over. It shows the week while the week can be told, else the last task while that can, else the week, and the while chosen is kept for as long as the card is drawn, as the sections are (R162). A course that moved is filled as far as the lower of where it stood and where it stands, and striped on to the higher, in the green of a gain or the coral of a step back, the stripes leaning the other way from right to left with the course. A move the words call one — forward, back, a rank up or down — is striped over a seventh of a step at least, at the edge of the step the course is under way in, so that the drawing never says less than the words beside it. Under the course a line says how the overall rank moved, in the move's colour after its arrow: a new rank, from one name to the other; forward; stayed; back a little; the rank below; and, of a while that cannot be told, that there is nothing to compare it with yet — never a word of the days with answers (R13). Each topic says what its own answers moved — a new rank, forward, back, the rank below, or a new topic, which stood nowhere before — and, where nothing moved, how its rank stands to the overall one: ahead, even or behind. By the title of the topics stands the count of those that moved up, a new topic among them, and of those that moved back, the same in words for a screen reader; at the head of the topics a legend names the stripes and the while. The arrows are hidden from a screen reader, which hears the line again whenever it changes. The rank's name keeps the text's colour, and the mastered mark stays. A move the card does not know, or cannot draw, draws nothing, and moves that do not read leave the progress whole. — *Why:* the mockup, and the author's answers of 2026-10-04: "even" as the mockup has it; the stripes at least 3 px wide wherever the words say a move; the rank's name not in its colour; the mastered mark kept. A seventh of a step is 3 px on the narrowest card, 320 px, and more on a wider one. The stripes are a pattern of each striped step's own drawing, named by the card so that no two drawings on a page share a name, since the host's policy promises nothing for a style set on an element and a fill takes no gradient. The switch is a group of radio buttons under a legend: a view gives no answer and sends nothing, so the arrows that move through a group change nothing anyone would regret, and the keyboard stops at it once. The coral of a step back reads close to the red of a wrong answer in the light theme, where the contrast text needs darkens it; R147 kept that red off the ramp, which a child lives with for months, while a step back is drawn for a while. — *Rejected:* the stripes at the exact width of the move, which draws a move of a point under a pixel while the words say the bar moved; the switch as two pressed buttons, which a screen reader cannot tell as one choice; a while that cannot be told locked out of the switch, which leaves the child guessing why; the rank's name in the colour of its course, whose yellow and lime fall short of the contrast text needs on white (R147); stripes drawn by a style or a gradient, which the host's policy does not promise. — *Consequence:* R147's rejection of a word for an even rank gives way; its rank's name in plain text stands. Two tokens, `--gain` and `--loss`, in both themes, held by a test to 4.5 to 1 against the card and 3 to 1 against the course's track. Twenty-seven keys are new in all 22 dictionaries. The payload, unchanged since T74.2, is read leniently: a while that does not read is one that cannot be told, and moves that do not read are none. The screen keeps the while chosen itself where nothing outlives it — the model's progress card, and a card drawn once on the site —, and the frame of a card keeps it for the progress over a task. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/design/progress.tsx` (`Segments` with a move, `MoveLine`, `MoveCounts`, `MoveLegend`), `controls.tsx` (`ViewSwitch`) and `mathtrail.css`; `web/src/widget/moves.ts`, `ProgressScreen.tsx`, `CardFrame.tsx`, `payload.ts` and `words.ts`; `internal/widget/tokens.css` and `widget_test.go`; `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts` and `web/src/widget/testing/lesson.ts`; SPEC 2.10, 7.3, 8.2, 8.12 and remark 38; RUN.md (T74.3).

**R176. The progress reviews the topics for the adult — the strong ones, the ones to develop and why, the ones too early to judge, and what to do next — by fixed rules, and every trap carries a sentence of advice.** *(Since T74.7 the last step may suggest a topic to begin, built on a strong one: R189.)* *(Since T74.5 the card draws the review: R180.)* *(Decided by the author in the planning of T74.4, 2026-10-04; carried out in T74.4, the fourth part of the progress after the author's mockup `draft/ui/profile/Рейтинг 4a.html`. The card draws the review in T74.5.)* `progress.ReviewOf` is a pure function of the profile, its summary, the map of the mistakes that repeat and that map's threshold. During the trial series there is no review. After it a topic is judged once it rests on five answers, as many as mastering it takes; one with one to four is too early to judge, which is no verdict on it, and one only skipped is not named. A topic is **strong** when it counts as mastered, or when its correction stands half a corridor or more above the overall level — half the step one rank takes, about 83 points; a rise of its correction by half a corridor or more over the week is one reason more, and never a reason by itself. A topic is **to develop** for any of five reasons: its correction half a corridor or more below the overall level; two wrong answers in a row, as many as lose its mastery; the hint used in at least half of its answers in the window, and in two at least; a trap its own answers in the window fell for as often as makes a mistake one that repeats; a fall of its correction by half a corridor or more over the week. One whose last answer was right has begun to move. A topic with a reason to develop it is never called strong, and only a topic within reach is named to develop. Each side names three at most: the strong ones mastered first, then the highest; the ones to develop with the topic the rule sets next first, when it is among them, then the lowest; ties in the catalog's order. Each topic to develop has a step, by what keeps it back: the advice of the trap it keeps falling for; for one that fell, two or three short tasks a day until it comes back; for one that stands low or was answered wrongly in a row, a few more tasks, set where the child stands; for one held back by the hint alone, each task tried first without it. One step more holds for every topic: the advice of the mistake that repeats most, unless a step gave it already — of mistakes that repeat as often, the first whose advice no step gave, since which was fallen for last says nothing of which matters more. The mistakes that repeat stay the map of R136, counted once. The pace of an answer, the tasks left without one and the task on the card are never read, and the rule that chooses a task reads nothing of the review. `get_progress` and `read_progress` carry it as `review` — `strong` and `develop`, each topic with its reasons by codes, its trap and whether it moves; `early`; and `steps`, each a kind with a topic and a trap — ids and codes alone, and absent during the trial series; their words for the model tell it in English, each step with its advice. Every trap of the catalog carries `advice`, one sentence in English for the adult, and the catalog does not load with a trap that has none; the package a task is written from shows each trap by its id and description alone. The description of `get_progress` and the instructions call it when someone asks what to work on, tell the review to the adult in plain words, never turn a topic too early to judge into a verdict, and practise a topic it names with `next_task`, that topic and a reason, once the child wants a task. — *Why:* the author's answers of 2026-10-04: five answers before a verdict; half a rank either way; to develop over strong, since the review is there to help; three on either side; the rule's topic first, so that the first step agrees with "Next up". The advice on rhythm is the author's decision, taken when the task was set. Every threshold is one the profile already has: five answers as mastery takes, two wrong in a row as lose it, the map's own for a trap. A move over the week is read by the size of the correction's move rather than by the word of SPEC 2.10, which turns on a whole percent of a rank, a point or two that any answer makes: one answer never moves a judged topic's correction half a corridor — its step is 0.32 at most from the fifth answer on, and narrows after each — so a fall or a rise takes two answers or more going one way. Topics out of reach are left out before the three are counted, so that no step sends the child to tasks too hard for them yet and no place on the list goes to a topic no step can be taken in. О-55 rejected extending the progress tool because every look at the progress would carry the advice into the model's context; that no longer holds: the card and the model read one payload (R97), the card needs the review to draw it, and what the words add is a short paragraph — the advice of four steps at most. The advice stays out of the package because every package of a request carries the whole trap catalog, and the advice is for the adult, not for the model writing a task. — *Rejected:* a seventh tool (О-55, T68.3), which would have the card and the model read two payloads for one screen; the week's word as the reason, above; a verdict on a topic from its first answers; a topic both strong and to develop; more than three on a side; a step that names a level and a difficulty, which the corridor of the topic sets (SPEC 3.4); the advice in the package; words in the payload, which the card will say in its own language. — *Consequence:* R136's rejection of strengths computed apart gives way: the strong topics are computed now, mastered or well above the overall level. R13 narrows: the review advises a rhythm — two or three short tasks a day — for a topic that fell, and nowhere else; there is still no counter, no streak and no reminder. О-55's tool and its catalog of outside material are gone, as RUN.md's phase 9 already said; the material is the site's topic pages (T74.6). A host that shows the model the payload in place of the words loses the advice, as it loses the descriptions of the mistakes; the words are what every host passes. `rating.CorridorWidth` is exported for the review, and the summary of the progress says which topics are within reach. The twenty sentences of advice are the author's to read line by line, and their translations are T74.5's. — *Where it lives:* `internal/domain/progress/review.go` and `progress.go`; `internal/domain/rating/corridor.go`; `content/catalogs/traps.json`, `content/catalog.go`, `content.go` and `package.go`; `internal/transport/mcp/progress.go` and `review.go`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; SPEC 1.3, 2.11, 7.3, 8.2 and 8.11; `docs/architecture/04-profile.md`; PRODUCT-V1 4.2, scenario 5, О-49 and О-55; RUN.md (T74.4, T74.5).

**R180. The card draws the review of the topics: a section after the topics, its parts under headings in their colours, its words the dictionaries', the English of its advice the catalog's own.** *(Since T74.6 the topic of a step is a link to its page on the site, where the host opens links: R186.)* *(Carried out in T74.5, 2026-10-04, the fifth part of the progress after the author's mockup `draft/ui/profile/Рейтинг 4a.html`. The author asked for the task to run on its own, so the choices below are the executor's, put to the author in the report.)* Once the trial series is over, the progress has a section "Review" after the topics, with "strengths and plan" by its title, as the mockup has it. Its parts stand in the order the adult reads them, each under a heading in its colour: the strong topics in the green of a right answer, the ones to develop in the coral of a wrong one, the topics too early to judge in the muted ink, the mistakes that repeat in the coral, and what to do next in the accent. A strong topic, or one to develop, is drawn after a dot in its side's colour — hidden from a screen reader, since the heading says the same — with its name, and under it a sentence for each reason it is named for, in the order the service gives them, and, for one to develop whose last answer was right, that a move has begun. The topics too early to judge are a line of names, as the card's language writes a list, with a note that there are too few answers to say how they stand, and no number: how many answers make a verdict is the service's to know. The steps are numbered, each the topic it is for, in the weight of a name, over what to do; the step for every topic has what to do alone. The mistakes that repeat move into the review once there is one; while the trial series runs, and from a progress before the review, they stay a section of their own before the latest answers. A part with nothing in it is not drawn, and a review with nothing to draw is no section (R162). What the card has no words for — a reason, a kind of step, a mistake's advice, or a mistake's name inside a sentence, that a later release adds — it leaves out rather than say wrong; a topic named for nothing it can say keeps its name, and a mistake that repeats is listed by its id, as every name the card lacks is (R136); a review that does not read is none, and the rest of the progress draws all the same. The words are 19 keys for the section, its reasons and its steps, and `advice.<id>` for each of the 20 traps, in all 22 dictionaries: English and Russian written for the task, the other twenty machine translations under R140's rules. A test holds the English advice to the catalog's, and the catalog's two sentences with straight quotes now have the typographic ones the dictionaries use. — *Why:* the mockup draws the section after the topics with its summary, its parts in this order and the steps numbered, the topic of each a link (T74.6). Its lines of why are free text, and the service sends codes: a sentence for each code reads in every language, where phrases joined into one would need each language's grammar. The steps put the topic over the advice rather than before it after a dash, as the mockup does, because a trap's advice is a whole sentence: after a dash it would start with a capital in English and a small letter in Russian, while a line of its own reads the same in every language, and it is the shape T74.6's link takes.
