"Too early to judge" is not in the mockup; it is the review's own part (R176), drawn as quietly as the mockup's muted notes. The mistakes stay a section of their own during the trial series because the review is absent then (R176), and they still show (R136). One English for the advice means the author proofreads one set of twenty sentences, and a change to the catalog's advice fails a test until the dictionaries follow — a reminder that their translations follow too. — *Rejected:* the steps after a dash, above; phrases for the reasons joined into one sentence, above; an English advice of the card's own beside the catalog's, which would need two sets read and could drift; the mistakes a section of their own beside the review, which the mockup puts inside it; an empty review drawn to say there is nothing to point out, a section with nothing in it (R162). — *Consequence:* R136's mistakes that repeat are a part of the review once the trial series is over, and R162's sections are five, the review between the topics and the latest answers. The README's pictures are taken again, the progress with its review. The site's sample progress, `site/data.json`, is past the trial series and carries no review, so the card on "Topics" folds the mistakes away in a section of their own, as a progress before the review is drawn; the file is the site's tasks'. The dictionaries have 275 keys. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/design/review.tsx` and `mathtrail.css`; `web/src/widget/review.tsx`, `ProgressScreen.tsx`, `payload.ts`, `names.ts` and `folds.ts`; `web/locales/`; `content/catalogs/traps.json`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts` and `web/src/widget/testing/lesson.ts`; the snapshots of `web/src/widget/rtl.test.tsx`; `docs/screens/` and `README.md`; SPEC 1.3, 7.3, 8.2, 8.12.1 and remark 38; RUN.md (T74.5).

**R186. A topic's name on the progress opens its page on the site through the host, at an address the card builds itself, and the words for the model name the pages of the review's steps.** *(Decided by the author in the planning of T74.6 and T74.7, 2026-10-04; carried out in T74.6, the sixth part of the progress after the author's mockup `draft/ui/profile/Рейтинг 4a.html`.)* `get_progress` and `read_progress` carry `site` — the origin the site is published on, `MATHTRAIL_SITE_URL`, and the languages it is written in — and each topic's `slug` and `site_page` from the catalog. The configuration names the origin as a browser writes it — the scheme and the host in lower case, a name in the ASCII form IDNA spells as a browser reads a host, without the checks of hyphens and letters a registry makes, no port the scheme implies — and refuses a host IDNA cannot spell, so that the consent screen, the words and the card name the site alike and the card, which holds an address to the exact origin, links what the words link; the tools are not built without it. The site's languages are those of its dictionaries, `en` and `ru`, which a test holds to `web/src/site/locales/`. On the card, a topic's name in Topics leads to the top of its page, and the topic of a step of the review to the part the step is about: `#traps` for a trap's advice, `#home` for any other; the step for every topic has no topic and no link, and nothing else on the card is one — the names of the strong topics and of those to develop, the topics too early to judge, the mistakes that repeat, and the profile's file in Drive, which stays named and never opened (R135). The card builds the address itself, from the origin — https, and only as a browser writes an origin —, its own language when the site is written in it and English otherwise, and a slug of the catalog's form, for a page the catalog marks published; it never opens an address the payload hands it whole, and nothing of the child is in it. Names are links only where the host said, as it connected, that it opens links, `openLinks`; a press, or the middle button, asks the host with `openLink` and keeps the browser from following the link itself, and a press while the host is still answering asks nothing. The browser's own menu opens the address as it opens any link's, where the host lets it: nothing a page can do stops it. A host that refuses, fails or never answers leaves a muted line under the name, "The chat didn't open the page. Its address:", and the address as text — left to right in every language, broken anywhere to fit, and taken whole at a touch —, the focus on the link, which stays, to try again. The line is said in a place under the name that is there, empty and polite to a screen reader, from the start, since a reader hears what a place it knows is filled with rather than a place that appears. The pages the host did not open are the progress screen's to remember while it is drawn; the components of the design keep none. The words for the model name each step of the review with its topic's page, in the language of the lessons the profile names, read as any tag is read, when the site is written in it — a longer tag read down to its language — and in English otherwise, and the instructions and the description of `get_progress` tell the model to pass a page on only as the result names it. Nothing turns the links off: they always lead to `MATHTRAIL_SITE_URL`, and `docs/self-hosting.md` asks a copy to publish its site from the commit it deploys. The preview's host opens a page in a tab of its own, and two scenes play a host that opens no links and one that did not open a page; `progress.link_refused` is in all 22 dictionaries, English and Russian written for the task, the other twenty machine translations under R140's rules. — *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-10-04: the pages in the language of the lessons for the model and in the card's own for the card, each in English where the site has no such language; the addresses in the words for the review's steps alone, where a page answers what the step asks of the adult; and no switch. The setting is required already, for the consent screen's links, and a link that a setting left empty would turn off is a link a copy loses without noticing, where a site published beside the service keeps it. An address built from a known origin, a pattern and a slug cannot send a child anywhere else, whatever a payload says, and https keeps a page from being changed on its way. A link the host would not open is a button that does nothing, and an address on the card is how a parent still reaches the page. — *Rejected:* an empty setting that turns the links off, which the task first asked for; an address in the payload for the card to open; a button that copies the address, which needs a permission to the clipboard the card does not ask for; links on every topic's name in the review; every topic's page in the words for the model, which the steps' pages make noise of. — *Consequence:* on a card that speaks the host's language rather than the lessons' — no language of the lessons chosen, or one the card has no words for —, the card and the words may link the same page in two languages. A host that does not say it opens links shows the names as plain text, and how Claude and ChatGPT answer is open (SPEC remark 67). A service run on this machine with an http site gives the model http addresses and the card no links. `Host`, the card's interface to its host, now has five methods, the most an interface here may have. — *Where it lives:* `internal/transport/mcp/site.go`, `progress.go`, `review.go`, `service.go`; `internal/config/config.go`; `tools/learners/go.sum`, `research/go.mod` and `research/go.sum`, which reach IDNA through the configuration; `internal/app/container.go`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; `web/src/widget/links.ts`, `bridge.ts`, `payload.ts`, `review.tsx`, `ProgressScreen.tsx`, `testing/`; `web/src/design/links.tsx`, `progress.tsx`, `review.tsx`, `mathtrail.css`; `web/src/site/StaticCard.tsx`; `web/src/preview/HostedCard.tsx`, `scenes.ts`; `web/locales/`; SPEC 7.3, 8.1–8.4, 8.12.2, 11.2, remark 67; `docs/self-hosting.md`; `docs/architecture/01-context.md`, `03-flows.md`; RUN.md (T74.6).

**R189. The review suggests one topic to begin, built on a strong one: the last of its steps, the topic a link and its base named in the sentence.** *(Decided by the author in the planning of T74.6 and T74.7, 2026-10-04; carried out in T74.7, the last part of the progress after the author's mockup `draft/ui/profile/Рейтинг 4a.html`.)* After the steps for the topics to develop and the step for every topic, the review may suggest one topic to begin: a topic within reach that the child has answered no task of and that builds, by the catalog's `builds_on`, on a topic the review names strong. The topic the rule sets next comes first when it is one; otherwise the first such in the catalog's order. Its base is the first of the strong topics, in the order they are named, that it builds on. A topic only skipped has no answer and may be begun — the one exception to the review's rule that a topic only skipped is not named —, since a task left without an answer says nothing of what the child knows (R98). The review reads the links through a catalog of its own, `progress.Catalog`, the rule's `tutor.Catalog` with `BasesOf` beside it, so the compiler still keeps every link out of the rule that chooses a task (R164); it has five methods, the most an interface here may have. `get_progress` and `read_progress` carry the step as `{"kind": "begin", "topic", "base"}`, and the words for the model say it after the topic's name and the address of the top of its page: "a new topic to begin; its base, Gaps and boundaries, is a strength." The card draws it last, the topic's name a link to the top of its page over a sentence that names the base in plain text, `review.step_begin`, the base at the head of the sentence and no verb after it — "Gaps and boundaries — a strength and a good base to start this new topic." —, so that no language has to decline the name or make a verb agree with a name in the plural, in all 22 dictionaries: English and Russian written for the task, the other twenty machine translations under R140's rules, each with the no-break space before a dash its dictionary uses. A card with no name for the base, or given a step to begin with no topic, leaves the step out, as it leaves out a mistake it has no name for, and a card of an earlier release leaves out the kind of step it does not know (R180). — *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-10-04: one topic, the rule's when it can be, so that the suggestion agrees with "Next up"; the new topic alone a link, since the base is a topic the child already does well, and two links in one step would be two pages for one thing to do. The base comes in the order the strong topics are named, which is the order the adult has just read them in. — *Rejected:* a topic to begin for every strong base, which would crowd out the steps for the topics to develop; the links read by the rule that chooses a task, which R164 keeps out of it; a link to the base as well. — *Consequence:* a step that begins a topic names a topic with no verdict on it, and its base, so SPEC 2.11's "a step names a topic and nothing more" names the base too. A strong topic past the three the review names is no base: the adult has not read it as strong. — *Where it lives:* `content/content.go` (`BasesOf`); `internal/domain/progress/review.go`; `internal/transport/mcp/review.go`; `web/src/widget/payload.ts`, `names.ts`, `review.tsx`; `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`; SPEC 1.2, 2.11, 7.3, 8.2, 8.3; RUN.md (T74.7).






**R131. The task card draws what the service handed it and recorded, locks whatever could answer twice, and sends to the chat what only the model can do.** *(Since T62.4 the card no longer turns to the waiting screen after "Another task": it keeps its task and says the new one will come below, R145.)* *(A message the host drops after "Another task" is superseded by R134: the card says at once what its deadline would say, with a button to ask again. The progress over the card is drawn as its screen rather than as it arrived: R135. The rest stands.)* The components of the approved design are ported to Preact in `web/src/design/`, with their names, their `mt-` classes and their states, and take their words from whoever draws them, so that the site can use them too. The five options are buttons rather than the export's radio inputs: a press gives the answer, and the arrow keys of a radio group — a keyboard's, a desktop screen reader's — would give one by moving through the group. An option or a button locked while an answer is checked ignores presses and keeps the focus it has; the card moves the focus only when it has been lost — to the one thing left to do, "Another task", while the replies read the result out, or to what the card says when nothing is left — and without scrolling. A task no longer being solved, answered on an older card, is closed with no button to ask for another: the task being solved is on a newer card, and asking for another from here would record it as skipped. The task's own words — its question, options, hint, trap and solution — carry the language it was written in and that language's direction, which need not be the card's. The result is drawn from the service's record — the choice, the right option, the trap, the solution, the rating before and after or, in the trial series, how far it has got — never from the press on this card, and an answer recorded before says so; "I don't know" shows the right option and the solution with no verdict, and the rating it cost. "Another task" turns the card to the wait at once, then sends its own label, in the card's language, as the child's message; a message the host drops is left to the adult and to the waiting screen. A question from the field goes to the chat in the child's words, and the card keeps it with "Sent to the chat", or "Not sent" with the words back in the field — never an answer. Whenever the card shows a recorded answer — its own, or one recorded before in the chat or in another card — the model is told one line, with the next message in the chat, of what is recorded rather than of what was just done: the task, the choice, whether it was right, the right option. An answer whose reply never came is one the card could not check, and says so rather than that nothing was recorded: sent again, it is told as the service recorded it. The line at the top reads the progress with `read_progress` inside the card, the task staying on the page, hidden, so that "Back to task" brings it back exactly as it was; until the progress screen is drawn, its payload is shown as it arrived. The card fills its container's width and takes the design's wide layout from `--widget-wide`, 640 px, measuring itself; the host's safe-area insets are the page's padding; the host's platform is not read, and hover colours are kept to pointers that hover; the host's style variables are not applied, so that the design's palette and fonts decide and the host decides only light or dark. A drawing is never wrapped or shrunk: one wider than the card scrolls inside its own frame, which the keyboard can reach, and a screen reader is told it is a drawing rather than read it character by character, since the wording carries every fact it shows. A rating is written as chess writes one, 1573, in the digits of the card's language, while every other number keeps the separators of its language. — *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-09-29, taken in the planning of T55 on the design's reference and the questions it left open: the export draws a demo that answers itself, and a card in a host has to hold one answer, one screen reader's reading and one width it does not choose. — *Rejected:* the export's radio inputs; dimming the buttons while an answer is checked, which the design does not do and which would flicker; a card capped at 640 px; a drawing that shrinks to fit, which makes its characters small on the phone it has to fit; the host's style variables over the tokens; ratings without separators through the general rule of the words, which would change every number; the navigation to the progress left to its screen's task, which would have to rebuild the card's state to return to it. A widget address that carries the page's version (SPEC remark 38) is left to the live runs of T62 and T63. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/design/`, `web/src/widget/TaskCard.tsx`, `TaskResult.tsx`, `lesson.ts`, `payload.ts`, `bridge.ts` and `words.ts`; SPEC 8.3, 8.4, 8.6, 8.8 and 8.12 and remarks 38 and 40; RUN.md (T55).

**R132. A solution is told in steps, one sentence each.** A solution arrives as one text — the model writes it as a paragraph, as every reference task does — and the card numbers its steps as the design does, cutting it into sentences by the sentence rules of the task's language, `Intl.Segmenter`, which know every script's full stop. Two rules of the card's own go on top, for the arithmetic those rules were not written for: a full stop, spaces and a number — a negative one too — start a sentence, since the rules read a full stop followed by a lowercase word as an abbreviation and find that word after the number, as in "…gaps. 4 + 1 = 5 posts."; and a piece that opens with an operation continues the one before it, since the rules end a sentence at the ellipsis of "1 + 2 + ... + 12". The rules read a hyphen or a colon after an ellipsis as going on and never cut there, but they do cut before the minus sign, which counts as an operation when a space follows it: against a digit it is the sign of a negative number, which may start a sentence. A tag the platform cannot read is cut by its default rules, and a platform with no sentence rules keeps each line whole. — *Why:* the author chose sentences over a change to the task's format. On the 603 reference solutions, the sentence rules alone leave 82 as one step; with the two rules, 37 stay one step — each of them one sentence — and no step is empty, begins with an operation or loses a character. — *Rejected:* one step per line, which asks the model and every reference task for a new way of writing; the solution as one paragraph, which departs from the design. — *Consequence:* a model that numbers its steps itself has its numbers shown beside the card's. The widget's tests hold every reference solution to the rules and generated texts in several scripts to the invariants. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/widget/steps.ts`; SPEC 8.12; RUN.md (T55).

**R133. The widget is looked at in a page that plays its host.** *(The approved design it was looked at beside was removed on 2026-10-03: the preview is the card's reference now, R156.)* *(Since T62.7 a browser in the image of R140 photographs the README's pictures from the preview's scenes: R150.)* *(It offers every language the widget speaks and the pseudo-language, and its scenes laid out right to left by hand are gone; a browser in a container now measures it, as a check rather than for the eye: R140.)* `just web-preview` serves `web/preview.html`, which frames the widget's own page, as it is being worked on, and drives each frame through the library's own host side over the messages between the frames: the host's context — theme, locale, width, insets — the scene's task, the service's answers to the card's calls, and the child's presses. The scenes are the states of a lesson, from the task to the result of each kind of answer, a question, the progress, the wait, the longest texts at the narrowest width and a layout mirrored for a language written right to left; they are drawn from the same data the tests pin, and a frame whose page scrolls sideways is marked. The address picks a scene, a theme, a language and widths, so that one view can be opened or sent as a link. The page is built into nothing and measured by no coverage. — *Why:* the author's decision: the check of the card is by eye, beside the approved design, and the states a live chat shows for a moment — an answer being checked — or not at all without a failure, cannot be looked at there; a page that plays the host shows the page a host runs rather than a copy of it. — *Rejected:* checking the card only live, which spends the author's subscriptions and cannot hold a passing state still; screenshots made by a browser in a container, a new image for what a browser the author already has does. — *Where it lives:* `web/preview.html`, `web/src/preview/`, `web/vite.config.preview.ts`, `web/package.json`, `justfile` (`web-preview`), `web/vitest.config.ts`, `sonar-project.properties`; RUN.md (T55).

**R134. A card that waits for the next task shows the usual course of one by the clock, says plainly when nothing is coming, and asks again only when told to.** *(Since 2026-10-05 the buttons under a task are two, the hint and another task: R208.)* *(Since T62.8 a card asks the service how its task stands, the rejection below no longer holding, and a full day is drawn on the card `next_task` draws: R152.)* *(Since T62.5 no card waits by the clock or asks again, and only a card a task is being handed in to gives up on its own; the day's limit stays as drawn here: R146.)* *(Since T62.4 the waiting screen has no warm-up, only a refused task draws it, and a card whose last attempt failed shows no steps: R145.)* The waiting screen is drawn in two places: on the task card, which turns to it as "Another task" is pressed, and on the card a refusal of `submit_task` draws. Both are framed as the task card is — the line at the top, which opens the progress over the card and brings the wait back as it went on, and the header with the grade — so the frame is one component, shared; over a card with no task the way back is "Back" rather than "Back to task". The field and the three buttons under a task stay in their places, switched off, and are one component on both cards, so that a card does not jump as it turns. The screen is the design's `GeneratingSteps`: the five steps of the draft, each done, under way or to come, its state named for a screen reader in words it does not show and heard as the list moves; the warm-up and the word of the deadline come into a place of their own beside the list, on the page from the start, so that each change is heard once. The steps move on a local timer — writing from 5 seconds, the answers from 35, readability from 50 — and claim no numbers, since the card cannot see the work; the last step, the task ready, is never claimed, because a card cannot see a task arrive and the new card says so by appearing. From 30 seconds a warm-up sits under the steps: one of nine short things to think about or do, with no question and so nothing to answer or check, in the dictionaries beside the card's other words, with a button for the next; the first is chosen by the clock, so that a child who waits often does not meet the same one first. At 120 seconds the steps and the warm-up give way to a message that cannot know whether a new card has come — "If no new task has appeared below, it isn't being prepared" — with what to do, ask in the chat or press the one button, "Ask again", which sends "Another task" once more and starts the wait over. A card whose ask the host refused says the same at once. A card drawn by the model's last failed attempt says the task did not work out and goes on waiting: the model is told to ask for a new task at once, and the deadline covers a model that does not; asked again from the card, it waits for that ask alone. A new waiting payload in a frame starts a new wait. A `waiting` payload refused for the day, `status: limited`, is drawn as the day's refusal in the words of SPEC 10.3 — what happened, when it clears, what can be done meanwhile — with nothing to wait for and nothing to press. A card moves the focus only within itself and only while it holds it: one drawn in the chat while the child types there leaves the focus where it is. A second press that comes before the card has redrawn is the same ask. — *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-09-29, taken in the planning of T56: the warm-up in the dictionaries rather than in `content/`, since it is words for the child and is translated with the card's other words; the day's refusal drawn from its payload, although no card receives one yet; the button to ask again kept, with its caveat; and a refused ask shown as the deadline at once. The timings put the steps inside the prototype's median of 69 seconds, and the deadline is 03-flows'. — *Rejected:* a card that asks the service whether the task has come, a Drive read per question (03-flows); dropping the button to ask again, which leaves a child with a stuck card nothing to press; a button to ask for another task on the card of the last failed attempt, which would be pressed while the model is writing the task it was told to ask for at once, and reach the chat after it, turning that task into a skipped one — the deadline's button comes only after the time a task takes, under the word to look below first, and the risk it keeps is the one the author accepted with it; a warm-up of reference tasks or tasks with answers, which PRODUCT 4.4 rules out. — *Consequence:* the limit's card is drawn by contract: `next_task` draws no card, so the card the child pressed "Another task" on still learns nothing of a full day, and its deadline speaks after 120 seconds (SPEC remark 61). A card the host draws again starts its wait over, having no moment to measure it from (SPEC remark 64). The waiting screen's words and its timings are the author's to read in the preview, where the scenes of a refusal, a refused ask, the last attempt and a full day are drawn. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/widget/WaitingCard.tsx`, `waiting.ts`, `CardFrame.tsx`, `LessonFoot.tsx`, `progress.ts`, `focus.ts`, `payload.ts`, `TaskCard.tsx` and `WidgetApp.tsx`; `web/src/design/blocks.tsx`, `icons.tsx` and `mathtrail.css`; `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`; SPEC 8.2, 8.3 and 8.12 and remarks 61 and 64; `docs/architecture/03-flows.md`; RUN.md (T56).

**R135. The progress, the profile and the first sign-in draw what their tools say, and whatever changes a profile is asked for in the chat.** *(Since T81 the ranks are told by their numbers rather than as places on a trail: R213.)* *(Since T74.6 the progress opens a topic's page on the site through the host, where the host says it opens links; the profile's file is still named and never opened: R186.)* *(Since T74.1 the progress folds its parts into sections, and opened again over a card it finds them as they were left, for as long as the card is drawn, though it is read afresh: R162.)* *(Since T62.7 the first sign-in only tells, with no tick and no button and a fourth note, "How to start"; "Edit" opens a form on the card in place of a message to the chat; and `get_profile` and `save_profile` draw no card, so the profile's own card is one a host draws only from an earlier chat: R148.)* *(Since T62.6 the progress is drawn after the author's mockup — a course of the ranks for the overall rating and for each topic in place of the pips and the topics' numbers, the parent's data under the profile on the progress too, and "Edit profile" at the profile's head: R147.)* *(Since T62.2 a lesson starts with `next_task`, which draws no card: a new adult is asked in the chat to say they are the child's parent or tutor, before any detail of the child, and the first sign-in's tick is met only where a tool that draws a card finds no profile; T62.7 makes that card one that only informs.)* The progress is one screen in two places: over a task or a waiting card, read with `read_progress` from the line at the top, and alone on the card `get_progress` draws, which has no line at the top since no task stands behind it. It heads with the child's pseudonym and grade, and puts the overall rating under "Rank 3 of 11 · River crossing": the rank's name is a key, `rank.1` to `rank.11`, a place on a trail up a mountain rather than a title a child is given, and eleven pips under the number show how far up it is. While the trial series runs, "3 of 5" stands in the rating's place, with five pips and no rank. Under it come what is next — the recommended topic, "once more" when the rule works it over after a mistake — the topics met with their ratings as plain numbers and a mark on the mastered ones, the five latest entries of the window marked right, wrong or skipped, how many tasks were left without an answer in all, and the child's profile for the parent: the grade, with a line that it is only a label now; the interests; what the child has not met at school yet, by the skills' names; and the language of the cards, named in the card's own language, or "the chat's". "Edit profile" sends its own label to the chat, and the card says whether the chat took it, and asks no more from that screen once it did; a progress opened again over a card is a screen anew. The words of that note are the chat's, `chat.sent` and `chat.not_sent`, which the questions of a task card say too. The profile's own card, drawn by `get_profile` and `save_profile`, shows the same details — never the parent's notes, which the card does not even read — and under them, on the card `get_profile` draws, whose tool says where the file is, the parent's data: where the file is, which is the export, and the other files that hold a profile, to delete; how to delete it; how to cut the service off in the Google account; how to remove the app. It names the file and the folder and opens nothing: a card opens no link, and the chat has the link. A change refused says nothing was saved, over the profile as it stays. The first sign-in says what MathTrail is, the rule of the pseudonym, what the chat will ask and where it is kept, and its one button, "Create a profile", which sends its own label to the chat, stays off until the adult ticks "I am the child's parent or tutor"; a first profile refused says it was not made. `submit_task` finding no profile draws the first sign-in too, as SPEC 7.3 has it, and `read_progress` finding none shows it over the card, and a reply that does not read says the progress did not load. Where the platform has no list formats, a list is set apart by commas, rather than the card failing to draw. The topics and the skills are named by keys, `topic.<id>` and `skill.<id>`, which a test holds every id of the catalogs to, and a name the card has no words for is its id. A payload no screen reads — a screen nobody draws, a field of the wrong kind — draws a card that says it cannot be shown and that the chat says the same in words, where the page used to show what arrived. A new payload in a frame starts its card afresh, and the same payload told again keeps it. — *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-09-29, taken in the planning of T57: one task rather than two; places on a trail for the ranks, since a title in Russian is a masculine noun and the model is asked never to show a child's gender; the tick before the button, which is what makes the adult's word a confirmation, though the chat can be asked in words all the same; the parent's data on the profile's card alone, where the tool that draws it says where the file is; and, on seeing both, eleven pips rather than none. A card that opens no link is SPEC 8.3's. — *Rejected:* titles for the ranks; a statement with no tick; the parent's data under the progress too, whose tool says nothing of the file; the whole window of twenty entries, too long for a phone; the payload shown as it arrived, which is a page of JSON for a child once every screen is drawn; the misconception map, which is T57a's. — *Consequence:* the model's instructions say what "Create a profile" and "Edit profile" ask. The words of the three screens, the eleven ranks, the seventeen topics and the twenty-five skills are the author's to read, and their translations are T59's. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/design/progress.tsx`, `controls.tsx` (`Checkbox`), `icons.tsx` and `mathtrail.css`; `web/src/widget/ProgressScreen.tsx`, `ProfileScreen.tsx`, `FirstRunScreen.tsx`, `CardRoot.tsx`, `CardFrame.tsx`, `ChatRequest.tsx`, `UnreadableCard.tsx`, `names.ts`, `payload.ts`, `words.ts` and `WidgetApp.tsx`; `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; SPEC 8.2, 8.3, 8.6 and 8.12 and remark 38; RUN.md (T57).

**R136. The progress maps the mistakes that keep coming back, counted over the history window, and an answer that makes one again says so.** *(Since T74.5 the mistakes that repeat are a part of the review once the trial series is over, and a section of their own while it runs: R180.)* *(Since T74.4 the strong topics are computed, as the review of the progress names them: R176.)* *(Since T74.1 the mistakes that repeat are a section of the progress of their own, before the latest answers: R162.)* *(Since T62.6 a mistake is drawn with a dot for each time, up to five, rather than a bar: R147.)* `progress.Mistakes` is a pure function of the profile's history window — its last twenty entries — and the catalog: every trap behind at least `MATHTRAIL_TRAP_REPEATS` answers of the window, with how many, the most frequent first and, of two as frequent, the one fallen for last first. Only a wrong answer to an option counts: "I don't know" chose no option, and a skipped task has no answer. A trap the catalog no longer has is not counted at all, as a topic it no longer has is left out of the progress, so that an answer never says a mistake repeats that the map does not list. `get_progress` and `read_progress` carry the map as `mistakes`, `[{"trap", "times"}]` — catalog ids and counts, nothing of a task or an answer —, empty when nothing repeats and with no profile, and their words name each mistake by the catalog's description. The progress screen lists them after the latest answers as the design's "Mistakes that repeat": each in plain words, `trap.<id>` in the dictionaries, which a test holds every id of the catalog to; how many times; and a bar as long as its share of the most frequent, drawn as a picture whose length is an attribute rather than a style, and turned over right to left. The block is not drawn when nothing repeats. `submit_answer` marks the trap of its result `repeated` when the answer it has just recorded makes the trap one that repeats — that answer is counted with the rest —, and its words then ask the model to end its explanation with one short reminder of that mistake, in its own words; the card says "This mistake has come up before" under the trap, with no number, and its line to the model asks the same. The service keeps no reminder of its own and translates none. The threshold is `MATHTRAIL_TRAP_REPEATS`, 2 by default: below 2 is refused, since a trap met once is a slip, and so is above 20, since the window could never hold it that many times. The two bounds are named once, the window's length in `profile` and the fewest repeats in `progress`, and the service refuses them when it is built, as the configuration does. What goes well has no aggregate of its own: the mark on a mastered topic and "Next up" say it already. — *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-09-30, taken in the planning of T57a: the window rather than the per-topic counts, which never forget, so that a mistake the child has left behind drops off the map with the answers it was behind; 2 as the default; "Mastered" as what goes well; and a line on the card under the trap, with no number. — *Rejected:* the per-topic counts the rule reads, which 04-profile had first named for the map — a mistake from months ago would stay on it for good; strengths computed apart, topics above the corridor or runs of right answers, which would say again what "Mastered" says; a number on the card's line, "the third time", which reads as a score kept against the child; a reminder written into the catalog or the dictionaries, which the model says better, in the chat's language and about the task at hand. — *Consequence:* the rule still reads the per-topic counts, so the trap the rule works over and the map can disagree, as they are meant to. Both fields are additions, and read both ways: a page a host kept from an earlier release reads the new payloads without them, and this page reads a payload from before them — a progress card of an earlier chat, drawn again — as one where nothing repeats (SPEC remark 38). `repeated` is worked out whenever the answer is told, from the window, which cannot change while the answer can be told again: only a threshold changed by a redeploy in between would tell it differently. The twenty names of the traps are the author's to read, and their translations are T59's. — *Where it lives:* `internal/domain/progress/mistakes.go`; `internal/config` (`MATHTRAIL_TRAP_REPEATS`); `internal/transport/mcp/progress.go`, `submitanswer.go`, `payload.go` and `service.go`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; `web/src/widget/ProgressScreen.tsx`, `TaskResult.tsx`, `lesson.ts`, `names.ts` and `payload.ts`; `web/src/design/progress.tsx`, `blocks.tsx` and `mathtrail.css`; `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`; SPEC 7.3, 8.2, 8.3 and 11.2; `docs/architecture/04-profile.md`; PRODUCT 4.2; RUN.md (T57a).

**R140. The widget speaks the twenty-two languages of v1, twenty of them in machine translations, and its layout is measured in real browsers in every one.** *(Since T81 the ranks have no names to translate: R213.)* *(Since R161 CI measures it in six parts at once, on six runners: on one, the check had grown to some twenty minutes.)* The dictionaries of SPEC 8.7 are `web/locales/<tag>.json`, each with the 189 keys of English. `en` is the source and `ru` was written beside it; the other twenty were machine-translated for T59 from the English, with the Russian beside it and a note for every key on who reads it, where it stands and how short it has to stay: the informal "you" to the child and the polite one to the parent; nothing that shows the child's gender — verbal nouns and "let's" in Arabic, impersonal forms in Polish, Ukrainian, Hindi and Urdu, no gendered particles in Thai —; the ranks as places; the mistakes impersonal. No speaker of those twenty languages has read them, and SPEC 8.7 says so; a dictionary a speaker has read moves to a row of its own there. A development build — the preview and the widget's tests — also speaks `en-XA`, a pseudo-language made from English as the build runs: every letter accented, every vowel doubled, and counting words added until each text is twice its English and ten characters more, in brackets, its slots and plural forms kept. The build that ships leaves it out, and the build's test holds the page to that. `just web-layout` runs the preview in the pinned Playwright image — Chromium and WebKit; every scene, in every language and the pseudo-language; 320, 360 and 640 px; the waiting scenes again after 35 and 125 seconds of a clock moved on — and fails on a page that scrolls sideways, a part that sticks out of its card, and text that runs out of its box or is cut short, all but the pseudonym at the top, which is meant to give way. The page's clock stands still but for what the check moves it on by, so that each card is measured at the moment it is meant to be, however slow the machine; and a run that measured nothing, or was asked for a language or a width the preview does not offer, fails too. CI runs it as a job of its own on the runner, since it needs Docker, and keeps what it found and a picture of every card in `ar`, `fa` and `ur` at 360 px. The markup of six screens in Arabic is held to Vitest snapshots. What the check found is mended: a note beside a name, "Not sent — try again" in Russian, ran 17 px out of the header of a reply at 320 px, and now goes under the name and wraps there; the badge that carries the grade ended in an ellipsis once it was twice as long, hiding the grade, and now goes on to a second line; and the send arrow of the question field turns round right to left, as the chevrons do. — *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-09-30, taken before the dictionaries were written: the layout is measured by a real browser rather than by eye alone, in the image T58 measured the drawings in; the snapshots are of markup, in the repository, and the pictures are not, so that the rendering of a font never turns a test red. The pseudo-language is twice as long because the first one, 40% and eight characters longer, came out shorter than the dictionaries on 59 keys, and too short to show the one overflow a real language did. Its tag is Android's own. It is made from English rather than kept as a file, so that it never falls behind the English, and never ships. — *Rejected:* a browser in the development image, run by Vitest in `just ci-web`, which puts half a gigabyte into the image every job pulls; the layout checked by eye in the preview alone, which catches what somebody happens to look at; pictures kept as references and compared pixel by pixel, which any change of font or engine would turn red; the pseudo-language as a file in `web/locales/`, which would ship; a badge that ends in an ellipsis, which hides the one fact it is there for. — *Consequence:* the widget's page grows from 323 KB to 543 KB, 151 KB compressed (SPEC remark 18). The check takes about eight minutes, most of it twenty-three languages in two engines. The tests that relied on Spanish and Portuguese being missing now use Swahili, which the widget does not speak. The translations are a machine's words for a child, and a speaker may find them stiff: T62 and T63 see them in the hosts, and a correction is an edit of one file. — *Where it lives:* `web/locales/`; `web/src/i18n/pseudo.ts`; `web/src/widget/words.ts`, `rtl.test.tsx` and its snapshots, and `testing/lesson.ts`; `web/src/design/mathtrail.css` and `controls.tsx`; `web/src/preview/`; `web/scripts/layout.ts` and `layout.test.ts`; `web/vite.config.widget.test.ts`, `vitest.config.ts`, `tsconfig.node.json` and `package.json`; `justfile` (`web-layout`); `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `.gitignore`, `.dockerignore` and `sonar-project.properties`; SPEC 8.6–8.8 and remark 18; CLAUDE.md; RUN.md (T59).

**R150. The README's pictures are the widget itself, photographed by a script from the preview's scenes.** *(Decided by the author in the planning of T62.7, 2026-10-03.)* *(Since R211 they are of the dark theme alone, which the README shows to a reader of either.)* `just web-screens` runs `web/scripts/screens.ts` in the pinned Playwright image of the layout check (R140): Chromium draws the preview's scenes "task", "wrong" and "progress, the model's card" in English, in the light and the dark theme, at 428 px and twice that density, and writes them over `docs/screens/`, the corners round the card left clear for a page of either theme to show through. It shares the driving of the preview — serving it, finding its cards, waiting on a still clock for each to settle — with the layout check, in `web/scripts/drive.ts`, and the check of the pins of Playwright with it, `_playwright-pinned`. A test holds the scenes to the preview's, the width to the ones the preview offers, and the files to the README. It is run by hand when a change of the cards deserves new pictures, and what it writes is looked at before it is kept; no check runs it. — *Why:* the author asked for the pictures to show the cards as they are: the old ones were exported from the approved design, with a question field and an "Edit profile" the cards no longer have. R133 rejected screenshots by a browser in a container as a new image for what the author's own browser does; the image has been there since R140, and what it gives a picture — the same browser, fonts and width every time, and no hand — is what makes one run like the next. — *Rejected:* pictures taken by hand from `just web-preview`, which drift with the hand that takes them; the script in CI, where a picture that moved by a pixel would fail a build no change of anybody's broke; pictures in every language, which the README does not show. — *Consequence:* the fonts are the image's, a Linux system's, rather than those of the phones a parent uses. — *Where it lives:* `web/scripts/screens.ts`, `screens.test.ts`, `drive.ts` and `layout.ts`, `web/src/preview/Preview.tsx` (428 px), `web/vitest.config.ts`, `justfile` (`web-screens`, `_playwright-pinned`), `docs/screens/`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`; RUN.md (T62.7).

**R191. MathTrail's logo and icons are its own: a coloured logo, drawn once as the site's icon and again in the card, and one set of outline icons.** *(Decided by the author in the planning of T61a and on its comparison page, 2026-10-04; carried out in T61a.)* *(Since R203 the card draws the logo for either theme: the site's icon in the dark theme, and in the light theme the same ribbon and star on a white tile under a hairline.)* *(R216: `serverInfo` names the site's icon, as this entry left to T71.2.)* The card's mark, the child's avatar and the card's icons came from the first export (R89), which had taken them from another app; drawings of the project's own take their place. The logo follows the author's picture: one ribbon folded into an M on a dark navy tile — the blue leg and the blue band down into the middle, the green band up out of it and the green leg, the bands over the legs and the green band over the blue one, showing a little through — and an orange four-point star over it; square, on a grid of 48, each part's colour running from its top to its bottom, the variant the author chose over a flat one. It is `site/assets/favicon.svg`: the site's icon and the mark beside its name in the header and the footer, at the address it always had (R143). The card draws the same logo in its header, at 32 and 28 px, since a card loads nothing: `Mark` names its gradients and its clip per drawing, as the stripes of R171 are named, and a test holds it to the file. The icons are one set: a grid of 24, lines 2.5 wide, rounded at their ends and corners and never filled, each in one colour — the text's, or the token of what it marks, `--correct`, `--wrong`, `--ink-strong`, `--track` or `--text-muted` — given as the drawing's attribute, never as a style. A verdict and a step are a ring around a tick or a cross rather than a filled disc; the avatar is a ring with a head and shoulders, the same for every child; the site's menu and chat are icons of the set. The tokens `--mark-fill`, `--mark-ink`, `--avatar-fill` and `--avatar-ink` are gone. The drafts in `draft/ui/site/` carry the logo where the old mark was, and drawings of the project's own where the borrowed icons were. A test of the widget's build holds the page it builds to none of the old drawings, named by the SHA-256 of every part of their paths, so that none of them is kept in the repository and an old part is found wherever it is drawn. — *Why:* the icons were another app's, and the product goes to the directories with its own. Claude's guidelines for MCP Apps (https://claude.com/docs/connectors/building/mcp-apps/design-guidelines, read 2026-10-04) ask for "monochromatic, outlined icons that match the host's icon color tokens" and allow "your own brand colors for accents and identity"; ChatGPT's (https://developers.openai.com/plugins/concepts/ui-guidelines, read 2026-10-04) ask for custom iconography that is "monochromatic and outlined". Claude shows a connector added by its address with the icon of the last two labels of its host, through Google's favicon service at 64 px (anthropics/claude-ai-mcp issue 838, 2026-08-12), and does not read `serverInfo.icons` (issue 152, open since 2026-04-06): MathTrail's host gives `mathtrail.app`, so the site's icon is the connector's. Google takes the icon the home page names and draws the SVG itself — on 2026-10-04 its service returned the old mark as a PNG of 64 px — and asks that the icon's address stay the same; it reads a site again in days to weeks. Where ChatGPT's developer mode takes an app's icon from is not documented, and is a live check. — *Rejected:* a monochrome logo, as the task first said: both hosts leave colour to an app's identity, and the author chose the coloured one; the mark taken out of the card's header, as ChatGPT asks — "Do not include your logo as part of the response. ChatGPT will always append your logo and app name before the widget is rendered" — which the author declined, so that T71.1 weighs the mark against the rules of the day; the card reading the file as text and writing it into its page, which would set markup from a string and rename the gradients of every copy by rewriting it, where a component of the card and a test that holds it to the file do neither; a ready-made set such as Lucide, whose licence allows it but whose drawings are not the project's; filled discs for a verdict; a PNG icon, or one served at `mcp.mathtrail.app`, while Claude reads the domain's and Google draws the SVG; `serverInfo.icons` now, which T71.2 adds and Claude does not read yet; the old paths written into the test as strings. — *Consequence:* the README's pictures and the site's sharing pictures are photographed anew (R150, R188); the connector's icon in Claude's list changes once Google reads the site again; T71.2 gives `serverInfo` the logo, and T71.4 draws it in the directories' sizes with a dark variant — the navy tile stands at about 1.1:1 against ChatGPT's dark #212121 and may want a lighter rim; the repository's history still holds the old drawings. — *Where it lives:* `site/assets/favicon.svg`, `web/src/design/icons.tsx`, `web/src/design/thread.tsx`, `web/src/site/Header.tsx`, `web/src/site/Chat.tsx`, `web/src/site/brand.ts`, `internal/widget/tokens.css`, `web/vite.config.widget.test.ts`, `draft/ui/site/`; SPEC 8.12; RUN.md (T61a).

**R194. The card of a task offers the choice of the topic once the trial series is over: a button at the end of its row, the choices under the row, and a mark above a task given on the topic chosen; a choice is saved, told to the model, and only then asked for as another task is.** *(Carried out in T78.2, 2026-10-04, the card's part of R193, after frame 5a of the author's mockup `draft/ui/next-task/topic-choice.html`.)* *(Since R197 the card saves a choice through the service its context gives, `serviceThrough` in `hostedService.ts` in a chat, and `edits.ts` is gone.)* The row of a task's card ends with "Topic:" and the topic the lessons are kept to, or "the coach chooses", before the answer and after it, its words wrapping rather than cut, its arrow turning up while the choice is open; it names the choice it opens (`aria-controls`) and says whether it is open (`aria-expanded`), as the hint's button does, and takes no press while an answer is checked or an ask is on its way. A card whose task comes with no `topic_choice` — in the trial series, of an earlier release, on the site — has no button, no choice and no mark. The choice opens under the row, in the same card: "Which topic next?", that the new task comes at once and the topic stays for the next ones, and a cross; Escape closes it too, and the focus goes to the choice in force as it opens and back to the button as it closes. It offers "The coach chooses", by the child's progress; "Suggested", the review's topics the payload names; and every topic of the catalog in the six groups of the site's page of topics, in its order, each group's name a link to its part of that page — `/<language>/topics/#<group>`, built and opened under R186 —, above its topics on a narrow card and in a column of 140 px on a wide one. The groups come with the build from `site/data.json`, and the lowest grade of each topic from the catalog, as the form's countries come from `places.json`; a topic whose lowest grade is above the child's grade is quieter and says from which grade, and can be chosen all the same, since the rule does not read the grade. The choice in force is pressed in (`aria-pressed`) and ticked. A choice is saved with `edit_profile` and `lesson_topic` alone, the empty text for the coach's; once it is saved, the service's words go to the model with `updateModelContext` when it changed anything — after the line of an answer on the card no message has carried yet, since the host keeps one line and a new one would drop it —, and the child's words go to the chat once the host has taken the line, or half a second later — "Another task — on the topic “Clocks”", "Another task — the coach chooses the topic" —, the button naming the new choice and the card saying the new task comes below (R145). A choice not saved is said under the choice, and nothing goes to the chat. While a choice is on its way the card is locked as it is while an answer is checked, and an ask or an answer under way locks the choice, pressed in the same moment too. A task on the topic chosen has "Topic: Clocks" above its wording while the choice stands, with a cross, the chip's own, that gives the choice back to the coach and asks for a task, as the coach's choice does; a task whose own topic is chosen on its card has none, and neither has a closed task. The card knows a task's topic was the choice only as `read_task` names the choice, so a card drawn again after its own topic was chosen does mark it. "Next up" on the progress says under the topic that it was chosen for the lessons. The words are twenty-one keys in the twenty-two dictionaries, the groups' names among them as `group.<id>`, in English and Russian the site's own — a test holds them to the site's —, and the other twenty languages machine-translated under R140. The README's pictures of a task and of a wrong answer are of a card after the trial series, with the button. — *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-10-04 (T78): the button only after the trial series, the review's suggestions, "The coach chooses" rather than the mockup's "under the AI's control", since a rule chooses and not a model, and a choice that stands until it is given back. The card saves before it asks, so that the task the ask brings is on the topic: an ask that raced the save could be answered with the rule's own topic. The model's line goes before the message, so that the model reads it with the ask and does not explain the choice. The groups are the site's data, so that the card and the page of topics show the same groups in the same order and a link leads to the part of the page of the same name; importing `groups` alone by name leaves the rest of the site's data out of the widget's page, which the build's test holds it to. The choices are buttons, as the answers are (R131): a choice asks for a task at once, and the arrows of a group of radio buttons would choose at every step. — *Rejected:* the choice as a dialog over the card, which a host's frame, the card's own height, would cut or scroll; the platform's select list, which holds no links, no suggestions and no grades; a choice that waits for the next "Another task", which the mockup's "the task changes at once" rules out; topics above the child's grade hidden or locked, a bar the rule does not have; the groups sent by the service with every task, for what changes only with a build; the mockup's anchors written from the Russian names, rather than the page's own. — *Consequence:* the widget's page carries the catalog of topics, some 4.5 KB, for the lowest grade of each. `Button` names the part it opens and takes a class of its own; `useLinking` moved to `web/src/widget/linking.ts`, and the form's call of `edit_profile` to `edits.ts` as `sendEdit`, both shared now; `useChatRequest` says whether an ask is on its way at the moment it is asked, and the task's card keeps the model's line that no message has carried yet. The image copies the catalog of topics and the site's data into the widget's stage, and `.dockerignore` lets the site's data through. The test host keeps the order of what it hears across kinds. The preview has nine scenes more, which `just web-layout` measures. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/widget/` (`TopicChoice.tsx`, `topicGroups.ts`, `TaskCard.tsx`, `ChatRequest.tsx`, `ProgressScreen.tsx`, `payload.ts`, `links.ts`, `linking.ts`, `edits.ts`, `names.ts`, `testing/`), `web/src/design/` (`controls.tsx`, `mathtrail.css`), `web/locales/`, `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`, `web/scripts/screens.ts`, `web/vite.config.widget.test.ts`; `Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore`; SPEC 8.2, 8.3, 8.11, 8.12.1; PRODUCT 3, 4.2; `docs/architecture/03-flows.md`; README; CLAUDE.md; RUN.md (T78.2).

**R201. The card of a task ticks off the checks and the task ready once the task has come, and only then turns into it.** *(Decided by the author on 2026-10-05, after lessons in Claude in which the card's course never got past the task being written.)* The card `next_task` draws still learns only what `read_task` tells it (R152): the topic picked, with the result; the task being written; a try turned down. While the task is awaited it claims neither the checks nor the task ready. Once the task is on the card, a card that waited for it ticks off the rest of the course before the task takes the course's place: the task written and its checks passed at once, the task ready 300 ms later, held for 500 ms more — under a second in all — with no step under way and none of the wait's news, a try turned down or the wait gone long, which the task has answered. A card whose first answer brings the task, one drawn again with an earlier chat, shows it at once, as before; a card whose questions went unanswered before the task came has shown the course all the while, and ticks it off. The preview has a scene in which the course is ticked off as the task comes, its first question answered with the task being written and the next with the task. — *Why:* the author's check: steps a card draws and never takes read as a card that stopped halfway. A task reaches the card only after every check has passed, so the two steps are true when the card ticks them: what the card cannot see is the moment they were taken, not whether they were. — *Rejected:* the two steps taken off the course, which hides the checks a task passed and leaves the course short of the task; a mark written to the profile as the hand-in starts, so that the card could show the checks under way — a Drive write more for every try, for checks that take a second or two and that a card asking every four seconds would mostly miss; the checks ticked a beat after the task written, which would show, for that beat, answers still to check that had been checked; the course ticked off only by a card that heard the task being written, which skips it for a card whose questions all went unanswered until the task came. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/widget/ComingCard.tsx`, `waiting.ts` and `TaskWait.tsx`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts` and `HostedCard.tsx`; SPEC 8.2.

**R203. The card's logo has a drawing of its own for the light theme: the same ribbon and star on a white tile under a hairline, the palest of their inks deepened; the dark theme keeps the site's icon.** *(Decided by the author on 2026-10-05, who found the dark navy tile too dark on a light card and chose "a white tile with a rim" of the five variants on a comparison page.)* `Mark` draws the logo twice, and the theme's tokens show one: `--mark-light` and `--mark-dark`, `block` or `none` in every block of the tokens, so that a host that names the theme and a viewer who prefers the dark one are told alike, bound to `.mt-mark-light` and `.mt-mark-dark` by the card's stylesheet. The dark drawing is still the site's icon element for element, which a test holds it to. The light one draws the same lines of the ribbon and the star, which a test holds alike, on a white tile 46.5 units wide whose 1.5-unit hairline, #d6dce6, stands that far in from the square so that all of it shows. Its blue leg keeps its ink; the blue band, the green leg, the green band and the star run through deeper ones (#5aa8f7 to #3b7fe3, #8fcf55 to #4fa648, #6fc35a to #3f9a45, #ffb938 to #ec8a12), so that their tops hold on white, and a test holds that no ink is lighter than its counterpart on the dark tile and that the palest is darker. The site's pages are always light, so the cards on them show the light drawing, while the site's header and its icon keep the dark tile. — *Why:* the author's choice. The tokens choose the theme for everything else on a card, and two drawings keep each one exact picture, the dark one the very file the site serves. — *Rejected:* one drawing whose colours are tokens, which would put a rim the site's icon does not have into the dark drawing and make its test resolve tokens before comparing it with the file; the page's other variants — a pale tile with the original inks, which wash out at their tops, the same tile with the deeper inks, and the ribbon with no tile; a light connector icon in Claude's list, which Claude takes from Google's favicon service as one picture for both themes (R191). — *Consequence:* the README's pictures of the light theme and the site's sharing pictures are photographed anew (R150, R188). — *Where it lives:* `web/src/design/icons.tsx` and `mathtrail.css`, `internal/widget/tokens.css`, `web/src/widget/__snapshots__/rtl.test.tsx.snap`; SPEC 8.12.

**R208. The task card has no "I don't know" button: a child who does not know says so in the chat, where the model records it as `?`, as before.** *(Decided by the author on 2026-10-05.)* Under a task not yet answered the card has two buttons, "Hint" and "Another task", and after the trial series the topic's button at the end of the row (R194). The card sends a letter alone, and its types say so: the service a card asks records a `Letter`, and only the reader of a result takes `?`. The answer `?` stays as R93 has it — a wrong answer, `confused` set, no option and no trap — and comes from the chat alone: the model's instructions tell it to record "I don't know" as `?`, even while a card shows the task. A card still shows such an answer, by the one way it reaches a card: an option pressed after it is told the answer recorded first, which the card draws as before — the right option, the solution with no verdict and the rating it cost — under "This task was answered before", and the model is told that line. The home page's demo carries a result for each option alone, since no press can reach `?` there, and the preview's scene of `?` is one said in the chat and told to an option pressed after it. — *Why:* the author's decision. — *Rejected:* taking `?` away with the button, which would leave a child who says it in the chat with no answer to give, and take from the profile, the reports and the instructions a case each of them counts; keeping a result for `?` in the demo's data, which no press can reach. — *Consequence:* a child stuck on a card has the hint, and "Another task", a skip that leaves the rating alone and that the adult sees (R98). The card does not tell the model that its hint was opened, so an "I don't know" said in the chat after it is recorded without the hint unless the child says so: the rating reads no hint, but the progress's count of hints in a topic and the reports' share of answers after a hint miss it. The counts of "I don't know" in the reports, and the share of it the study plans to follow, now hold what children say in the chat alone, and fall from this release for that reason rather than for any change in how they learn. The README's pictures of a task are photographed anew. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/widget/LessonFoot.tsx`, `TaskCard.tsx`, `choices.ts`, `lesson.ts`, `service.ts`, `hostedService.ts` and `payload.ts`; `web/locales/`; `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`; `web/src/demo/data.tsx` and `web/src/site/home.ts`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; `README.md` and `docs/screens/`; SPEC 2.2, 8.2, 8.3 and 8.12.2; `docs/architecture/03-flows.md` and `04-profile.md`; PRODUCT 3, 4.2 and О-33.

**R211. The README's pictures are of the dark theme alone, and the README shows them to a reader of either.** *(Decided by the author on 2026-10-05, on seeing the README on GitHub in its light theme, with the light pictures.)* `just web-screens` photographs the scenes of R150 in the dark theme only, and the README shows each as a plain image rather than as a picture that follows the theme of its page; the light pictures are gone from `docs/screens/`. The README says that the card follows the chat's light or dark theme and that the pictures show the dark one. The test of the pictures holds the README to every picture written and to no other, so that no lighter picture is left for a page to choose. The preview's page names the colour scheme of its theme, as a chat in that theme does: a browser draws a frame whose page is of another scheme than the page around it on an opaque backdrop of its own, #121212, and the dark pictures had carried that backdrop in the corners round the card, unseen on a dark page and a dark square on a light one. The three pictures are photographed anew, of v0.3.0, with their corners clear and the topics' links plain, as the card draws them now. — *Why:* the author's choice. — *Rejected:* pictures that follow the theme of the page (R150), which show a reader of a light page the light card; both themes photographed and the light pictures kept beside the dark, where no page shows them; the backdrop taken away by the photographing alone, which would leave the preview unlike a chat in the dark theme. — *Consequence:* a reader of a light page sees a dark card on it, its corners clear. The row of the themes in `docs/catalogs.md` points at the preview, which shows both, rather than at `docs/screens/`. — *Where it lives:* `web/scripts/screens.ts` and `screens.test.ts`, `web/src/preview/preview.css`, `docs/screens/`, `README.md`, `justfile` (`web-screens`), `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/catalogs.md`.

**R213. A rank is told by its number, and under the overall course the progress marks, for the parent, which grades' tasks each run of ranks roughly matches.** *(Decided by the author on 2026-10-05, after the first parents found the ranks' names meaningless, with the mockup `draft/ui/profile/Рейтинг 4a v2.html`; carried out in T81.)* The progress heads with "Rank 3" over "of 11", the next rank is "rank 4", a topic's rank is "Rank 4" at the end of its row, and a move goes from rank 2 to rank 3: the eleven names of the dictionaries are gone, and so is every key of them. Under the overall course, from the end of the trial series, three brackets mark the runs of ranks each level's grades roughly match, the run of the rank reached drawn stronger, with a note for the parent under them: the scale shows roughly whose tasks the child manages, the tasks are olympiad ones, and it is not a school mark. A level's run begins at the rank its children start in — the youngest level's at the first rank — and ends where the next begins: ranks 1–4 grades 1–2, 5–7 grades 3–4, 8–11 grades 5–6 (SPEC 2.4). `rating` works the runs out of the ladder and the ranks, `GradeLevel.FirstRank` and `LastRank` with `GradeLevelOfRank`, and the progress sends them as `overall.grades`, which the words for the model name too; the model is told the marks are for the adult, never a verdict on the child. A card that reads no runs, or runs that do not take the ranks one after another from the first to the last, draws no brackets. A card sets no style on an element, so the runs are the cells of a table one column a step wide, each spanning its run's steps, and a bracket gives up two pixels on each side it shares with another, which keeps its ends within a fraction of a pixel of the steps' edges. — *Why:* parents read "River crossing" as nothing, and asked where their child stands against the school years; a number keeps the steps the child climbs, and the brackets give the adult the years without telling the child they are behind. — *Rejected:* the school grade in the rank's place, which the ladder cannot tell apart within a level — grades 3 and 4 start at one point — and which would tell a child at the top of the screen that they are behind; the mockup's runs 1–3, 4–7 and 8–11, which match no rule of the ladder and put rank 4 under grades 3–4 though their children start in rank 5; a table of runs written in the code, which the ladder moved by the live runs would leave wrong; the names kept beside the numbers. — *Consequence:* R135's reason for places on a trail, that a title in Russian is a masculine noun, no longer arises. The README's picture of the progress is photographed anew. A host that keeps an older card shows the names until it is reloaded. — *Where it lives:* `internal/domain/rating/scale.go`; `internal/transport/mcp/progress.go`; `content/instructions/mcp_instructions.md`; `web/src/design/progress.tsx` (`GradeLegend`) and `mathtrail.css`; `web/src/widget/ProgressScreen.tsx`, `names.ts` and `payload.ts`; `web/locales/`; `web/src/widget/testing/lesson.ts` and `web/src/preview/scenes.ts`; `site/data.json` and `site/progress_test.go`; SPEC 2.4, 7.3, 8.2, 8.6, 8.7 and 8.12.1; PRODUCT 4.5 and О-48.

## Configuration

**R18. Viper reads the configuration, and nothing but the environment feeds it.** `internal/config` declares every variable once — name, default and type — and unmarshals it into `Config`; `Validate()` then refuses with a message that names the variable. No file, no remote source and no flag is registered. — *Why:* the author's decision, reversing the earlier "no Viper" note in CLAUDE.md. The table of SPEC 11 grows to around twenty variables as the sign-in, the limits and the solver arrive, and hand-rolled parsing grows a branch per type as it goes; Viper keeps that in one declaration and matches how the platform's other services read their configuration, so a person moving between them reads the same code. — *Rejected:* `os.Getenv` with hand-written parsing, which is what this replaced; registering a config file "for later", which would quietly break the rule that a deployment is configured by its environment alone (PRODUCT 6). — *Consequence:* an empty variable reads as unset, because Viper's `AllowEmptyEnv` stays off — the one behaviour worth knowing when a deployment sets a variable to the empty string and expects it to override a default. — *Where it lives:* `internal/config`, SPEC 11, CLAUDE.md ("Config").

**R19. gin serves the HTTP surface, and it stays in `internal/transport/http`.** The router, the handlers and the middleware follow the shape of the platform's other services: a handler is a struct with its dependencies and a constructor, a route is one line in `NewRouter`, an error answer is `apierror.Response` wherever no protocol prescribes a shape of its own (OAuth answers with `error` and `error_description`, JSON-RPC with its error object), and every request passes a request id, a panic recovery and a request log. — *Why:* the author's decision. The surface grows to the MCP endpoint, five OAuth endpoints and three metadata documents, each with its own middleware needs; routing groups, parameter binding and one recovery path are worth more than the standard multiplexer once that is true, and a person moving between this service and the others reads the same code. — *Consequence:* the domain packages must stay free of it — the linter denies `gin` and `net/http` inside `internal/domain`, which is the one place this service deliberately differs from the reference. An `http.Handler` from the MCP SDK mounts with `gin.WrapH`. — *Rejected:* `net/http`'s multiplexer, which is what this replaced; handlers inside domain packages, as the reference does it. — *Where it lives:* `internal/transport/http`, `.golangci.yml`.

## Limits and storage

**R15. A ceiling on failed generations, separate from the daily limit.** *(Set in T52: `MATHTRAIL_DAILY_FAILED`, five by default, checked in `next_task` beside the tasks of the day — R121.)* `daily.failed` in the profile counts requests that ended in `attempts_exhausted`, and `next_task` refuses with `limit_reached` once it reaches its ceiling — five a day by default, the number set in T52. — *Why:* О-35 deliberately makes a refusal cost nothing, which alone leaves a model that keeps failing the checks free to loop — three refusals, a new request, three more — with only a per-instance rate limit in the way (О-24). Each round costs the family a turn of a free-tier chat limit and six Drive calls; it costs us no tokens at all, since the service makes no LLM calls (PRODUCT 4.3). Found in the external review of 03-flows. — *Rejected:* charging the daily limit for refusals, which would redefine О-35 and make a bad model cost the child their lesson; leaving the loop to the rate limit alone. — *Where it lives:* SPEC 5.10, [03-flows](architecture/03-flows.md), [04-profile](architecture/04-profile.md), T43, T52.

**R17. The profile is recovered through pinned revisions, and pinning has two triggers.** *(Amended by R119: Drive lets go of a revision kept forever only by deleting it, so the first write of a day alone is kept, the earliest past a hundred are deleted, and a recovery keeps each revision it reads.)* A damaged file is recovered from at most five revision bodies: the four newest **plus the newest pinned one**. Pinning fires on every fiftieth write by our own `revision` counter **or** on the first write of a new day, seen from the `updated_at` already in the file. — *Why:* Drive purges unpinned revisions at a hundred, which is about a day and a half of use, so the pinned snapshot is the one guaranteed to exist — and a recovery that only looked at the last five would step straight past it after six broken writes in a row. The calendar trigger exists because a write lost in the write race takes its revision number with it, and a trigger that fires only on an exact multiple can miss. Found in the external review of 05-storage. — *Rejected:* pinning every write (200-pin ceiling, and the parent's storage quota); a deeper recovery window (more bodies fetched on a path that is already rare). — *Where it lives:* [05-storage](architecture/05-storage.md), T51.

**R80. The store is one contract: an explicit account, a revision only the store reads, and an export that is a place.** *(R128: a newer build's file past a rollout's time is refused as unsupported, and a new start is taken over any newer file.)* *(T51 kept `Load` as it is: a damaged file put back is told as `ErrRestored`, and two files by the export. The fifth operation is `StartOver`, and restoring from the bin stays the parent's, in Drive — R118 to R120.)* `internal/store` declares `Storage` with four operations; a fifth is agreed for T51, below. `Load` answers the profile and the revision it was read at. `Create` keeps the first profile of an account, and refuses an account that has one, readable or not. `Save` writes only over the revision it was computed from, and never creates. It also takes only a profile whose own `revision` is one past the stored one, which is the touch every write carries: 05-storage pins the history by that number and notices an early read by it, and the store is the one place every write passes through. A store checks this without reading the file again, since the revision it handed out can carry the number. `Export` answers a `Location`: the folder, the file and a link. There are four refusals: `ErrNotFound`, `ErrConflict`, `ErrCorrupted` and `ErrAccessRevoked`. Every call names its `Account`, made by `NewAccount` from the identifier the sign-in derived and the Google access token that opens the parent's Drive. The token sits behind a pointer that nothing outside the package can name, so no printing at any depth and no JSON reaches it, and an account prints as its identifier in whatever verb it is asked for. Accounts cannot be compared: two made alike hold different pointers, so the compiler refuses `==` rather than let it answer false. Two refusals come before anything is looked at. An account that names no one is refused by every operation, whatever its token would open, so that a call which lost its identity on the way in cannot reach anybody's profile. A profile that breaks its own rules is refused wherever it was aimed. A `Revision` is a string that only the store that issued it compares. Damage is decided once, in `store.Parse`, for every store. A file that is not JSON, is not a profile, or has an older shape this build has no way up from is `ErrCorrupted`, with its cause beside it. A file from a newer build is not damage and keeps `profile.ErrNewer`. The store in memory keeps the bytes `profile.Marshal` writes and reads them back through `store.Parse`, so it validates, copies and classifies exactly as the store in Drive will; checking a revision and writing are one step under its lock. `internal/store/storetest` holds every store to the same cases. The contract lets two racing saves both land, because a store that can compare revisions only before it writes cannot promise more ([05-storage](architecture/05-storage.md), "Two tabs"). — *Why:* the tools arrive before the store in Drive, so the operations they are written against have to hold once Drive is behind them. Starting over in place of a file nothing can read becomes a fifth operation, as agreed with the author after T40. It arrives with the store in Drive in T51: the damaged file is set aside and never deleted, and it is marked as set aside instead of as the profile. A new profile takes its place, both stores implement the operation, and `storetest` holds them to it. Whether restoring a file from the bin at the parent's request is also an operation of the store is left for T51 to decide. Until then `Create` refuses to replace any file at all. The credential travels in the signature and not in the context, because the context would hide what a call cannot work without. The export is the file itself, as 05-storage has it, and a store with nothing to open answers the zero location. Keeping bytes rather than a pointer makes the store in memory faithful at no cost, and a single shared contract is what makes the two stores interchangeable rather than merely similar. — *Rejected:* a user identifier alone, with the token read from the context. An export that hands over the file's contents, which would be the second copy 05-storage rules out. A result struct in place of the three results of `Load`: the notices T51 needs ("two files were found", "restored from the revision of that date") may extend `Load` then, and a mechanical change at every call is cheaper than a struct that nothing fills yet. Running the suite a second time so that its own lines count as covered: the lines that report a failure never run in a passing test. `storetest` is test code in a package of its own: `codecov.yml` leaves it out of the coverage, and `sonar-project.properties` counts it among the tests, as it does a `_test.go` file. The total that `just ci-test` prints still counts it, because Go reports a package without tests of its own as uncovered. — *Where it lives:* `internal/store`, `internal/store/memory`, `internal/store/storetest`. T43–T45 use it. T50–T51 implement it for Drive, add the fifth operation with its case, and add revoked access to the suite.

**R115. Drive is reached through a thin client of the service's own, and the store of profiles in Drive stands on it.** `internal/infra/drive` makes the five calls the store needs — a search by one of the service's own properties, the name of a file, what a file holds, a new file or folder, and new content for a file — each as one request to Drive's HTTP API v3, with the parent's token, asking for the fields the caller reads and no others, within `MATHTRAIL_DRIVE_TIMEOUT` (10 s by default, SPEC 9.2), following no redirect and reading at most 1 MB of an answer.
