The demo is one module file; without it every step of a lesson keeps its own card, and the button that copies the connector's address appears only when the script runs. The map of how the topics link lights up with CSS alone; were that not enough, putting "Topics" on the list would be a decision of its own. A page weighs at most 300 KiB with everything it can load: its HTML, its stylesheets, every file they reference — every font subset they declare, as if all were fetched — its scripts and its images, uncompressed. The home page and its demo are held to the same limit, and raising it would be a decision of its own (T66a). The checker counts the files a stylesheet references from T75.2 on, once it is written in TypeScript (T66c). — *Why:* a parent who opens a page with scripts off, or before they run, still reads all of it, and the limit keeps a page from growing heavy unnoticed; the fonts are the one thing a page loads that the checker does not see today. — *Rejected:* a limit of its own for each page, which the checker could learn only from something beside the directory it reads (R25); a script on every page for small comforts. — *Where it lives:* `web/scripts/prerender-site.test.ts`, the site's checker (`web/tools/sitecheck/` from T66c); SPEC 8.12; RUN.md (T75.2, T66a, T66c).

**R164. A topic carries the address of its page, the topics it builds on, and whether its page is out.** *(Decided by the author in T75.3, 2026-10-04.)* Every topic of `content/catalogs/topics.json` has a `slug`, `builds_on` and `site_page` beside its id, name, description and grade levels (SPEC 1.2). The slug is the last part of the page's address, `/<locale>/topics/<slug>/` (R158): the topic's English name in lowercase words joined by dashes, shortened where the name is long — `figures-on-a-grid`, `winning-strategy` — and once the page is published it never changes. `builds_on` names the one or two topics whose solid mastery is a good base to start this one, and a foundation says `[]`. `site_page` turns on with the task that publishes the page. The links are the author's, read against the reference tasks: the traps two topics share, the solvers' methods and frames they share, the tasks of one topic that lean on another's idea. They make 17 links in three layers — the six foundations (ordering, enumeration, gaps, clocks, parity, arithmetic with a trick), six topics one link up and five two links up. The loader refuses a bad or doubled slug, a missing `builds_on`, a base the catalog does not have, a topic that builds on itself or names a base twice, and a circle, which it names link by link; tests on the catalog as it ships hold a base to being taught no later than its topic, and the chains to three layers. The site's map, a topic's page, the card's links (T74.6) and the review's suggestion of a new topic (T74.7, R189) read the fields; the choice of a task does not (SPEC 3.1), nor does the package the model is handed. — *Why:* only `content/` is embedded in the binary, and the widget and the site read the same JSON, so one file is the one source the service, the card and the site agree on. A flag per topic is enough because every page exists in every language of the site (R158). The links are written per topic rather than worked out, because which topic is a good base for another is a teacher's judgement the data only supports. — *Rejected:* the fields in the site's data alone, which the card and the service cannot read; a list of the languages each page exists in, which R158 makes redundant; a slug made from the id, which would give `pigeonhole.basic` the address `basic`; the links in code; layers typed by hand, which drift from the links; the rule reading the links, which would change how tasks are chosen without a decision of its own. — *Consequence:* games builds on enumeration and parity, though remainders solve half its reference tasks: a link from divisibility would make a fourth layer through calendar and divisibility, and the map draws three. Clocks stands among the foundations by the author's choice, though four of its tasks count gaps. The published addresses stay a list written by hand (R158), never derived from the slugs. — *Where it lives:* `content/catalogs/topics.json`, `content/catalog.go` and its tests, `content/content_test.go`; SPEC 1.2, 3.1, 8.12.2; RUN.md (T75.3, T75.4, T75.5, T74.6, T74.7).

**R167. The site's frame: its menu and its footer are lists in code, and on a narrow screen the menu folds behind a disclosure.** *(Decided by the author in T75.2, 2026-10-04.)* *(Since R188 the menu opens with two sections of the home page, how a lesson goes and connecting, and the frame has one button the header shows, "Add to Claude", which leads to connecting and is left out below 720 px.)* *(Since R183 the footer names the page about who makes MathTrail before the documents, and the contact address and the link to the code live in `web/src/site/brand.ts`, which the footer and that page share.)* *(Since R198 the page of the coach is the menu's third entry, right after connecting, and the menu folds below 1200 px rather than 1100, where its seven entries stop fitting in a row in Russian.)* *(Since R202 the menu names pages alone, the page of the coach first, and folds below 992 px.)* *(Since R209 the menu has "Research" before "About", the footer names it first, and the menu folds below 1100 px.)* The header's menu and the pages the footer leads to are written in `web/src/site/frame.ts`. An entry of the menu is a page, or a section of one, under a word of the site's dictionaries, the page being read marked; the footer names pages, each under its own title, before the code and the contact address. A page joins either list with the task that publishes it, and the build refuses an entry whose page the site does not have; the menu stays empty until "Topics" (T75.4). Below 1100 px, the draft's width, the menu folds behind a round button with three lines: a `<details>` element whose summary is the button, named by a word a screen reader says, and whose list drops under the header. The links are drawn twice, inline for a wide screen and inside the disclosure for a narrow one, from the one list, and the stylesheet shows one of the two. The footer writes the contact address out as a `mailto:` link, and a test of the build holds it to the address the privacy policy names. Every page the site serves is on the checker's list of published addresses, and the checker refuses one that is not. — *Why:* the author's choices of 2026-10-04. A disclosure opens with no script in every browser, and tells a screen reader it is a button and whether it is open; drawing the links twice costs a few lines of markup and cannot drift, since both come from one list. An address written out reaches a reader with no mail program set up, who can copy it. The list of published addresses is written by hand so that a page dropped from the build is noticed (R158), and a page that never joined it could be dropped unnoticed, so serving one is the finding. — *Rejected:* the draft's own handling, which hides the menu below 1100 px and leaves a phone without one; a second row of links under the header, which grows to three rows on a phone and can no longer stay on top; links that scroll sideways, as the draft's inner pages have them, which show one or two of five at 320 px and hide that there are more; a `popover`, which needs no second copy of the links but is new enough that an older Safari would show none of them; the word "Contact" in place of the address, which leads nowhere without a mail program; a footer of every page, which seventeen topics would bury. — *Consequence:* a page's task adds its entry to the menu, its word to the dictionaries and its addresses to the published list in one change, and T66b adds every page of twenty languages to the list. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/site/frame.ts`, `web/src/site/Header.tsx`, `web/src/site/Footer.tsx`, `web/src/site/style.css`, `web/tools/sitecheck/check.ts`; SPEC 8.12.2; RUN.md (T75.2, T75.4, T66a, T72.13, T66b).

**R170. A card of the widget on the site is drawn still from the widget's own screen, and the map of the topics lights with CSS alone.** *(T75.4, 2026-10-04: the author chose the groups of topics and approved the rest in the plan.)* *(Since R197 a card on the site has no way to the progress: its top line names the child alone, and the test holds a card on the site to a chat's but for that line.)* *(Since R188 the card of a task being written is drawn through the seam as well, as a chat draws it before the service has said anything of the task.)* *(Since R178 the card of a task is drawn through the same seam from a starting state of its lesson, which the task card takes for it: "the widget's code does not change for it" holds for the progress.)* The seam is `web/src/site/StaticCard.tsx`: it draws the widget's own components — the progress screen inside the card's root — with the widget's own words in the page's language, from the site's data, `site/data.json`, completed with the child's name in the page's words, and read by the widget's own reader of a progress, so that data the widget could not draw stops the build. The sections a page asks for are open, and the folded ones stay in the markup hidden, as in a chat. A page is drawn before anybody reads it, so the card is drawn at the narrow width a phone's chat gives it, and nothing on a page answers its buttons, so the card is `inert`. Its stylesheet, `/assets/card.css`, is built from the widget's own and loaded only by a page that says it draws a card. The widget's code does not change for it, and a test holds the card on the site to the card a chat draws for the same data: the same text, and the same elements in the same order. The map of the topics is laid out when the site is built, in fixed sizes (`web/src/site/topicmap.ts`): a column for each layer, from left to right in every language as a drawing is, the foundations in the catalog's order and every later column by the mean row of its topics' bases; a curve joins neighbouring columns, and a link that skips a column runs down a gutter, along a lane of its own under every box and up the next gutter, as the draft draws it. Pointing at a topic, or reaching it with the keyboard, lights every topic below it and above it, the lines among them, and its line of words under the map, by rules the page's head carries, written from the catalog's links with `:has()`; the page runs no script, and a browser that cannot read the rules shows the map unlit. On a narrow screen the map scrolls sideways inside its panel, as in the draft. The groups the topics are shown in are the site's data, every topic in exactly one; the pills over them are links to the groups, and a group's name is a word of the site's dictionaries, since a topic's page names its group too. — *Why:* the task asks for the widget's real screen on the page, drawn without a script, by one seam the cards of "Why" and of the home page will use too; a copy of the card would drift from the widget the day the widget changes, and the test that holds the two together is what makes the copy unnecessary. The draft measured its boxes in the browser and lit them with a script, and the site's pages run none (R160). — *Rejected:* the draft's script for the light and its measuring the boxes in the browser; a map drawn wholly in SVG, whose text cannot wrap a long name; a copy of the card in the site's look (R156); `WidgetApp` on a stand-in bridge, which opens the progress with every section folded, so that showing the topics would mean changing the widget; the rules inside the map's SVG, where a parser that reads the page in the tests stops reading the rest of it; a filter that hides groups, which needs a script. — *Consequence:* a change of the widget's progress screen shows on the site with the next build. The page's HTML carries the card's folded sections too. T75.10 and T66a draw their cards through the same seam. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/site/StaticCard.tsx`, `web/src/site/TopicsPage.tsx`, `web/src/site/TopicMap.tsx`, `web/src/site/topicmap.ts`, `web/src/site/topics.ts`, `web/src/site/data.ts`, `site/data.json`, `site/content/*/topics.yaml`, `web/vite.config.site.ts`; SPEC 8.12; RUN.md (T75.4, T75.10, T66a).

**R172. A topic's page is one template; its traps are counted rather than chosen, and every example's answer is proved by a solver of its own.** *(Decided by the author in the planning of T75.5, 2026-10-04: the proof a lasting test rather than a check made once, and "Add to Claude" waiting for the home page's section on connecting.)* *(Since R188 the ask of a topic's page has its "Add to Claude", which leads to the home page's section on connecting.)* *(Since R181 the examples of the page of the techniques are proved the same way, their solvers kept in `site/solvers/techniques/`.)* One component, `web/src/site/TopicPage.tsx`, draws every topic's page. The registry of pages is built from the catalog and holds it under `topics/<slug>` for every topic; which topics have a page is for their words to say and for `site_page` to agree with (R164). The words are a closed set of blocks: the first screen; the ground the topic stands on, as cards; its key idea, as a table and a note; the steps of the move; the examples; the traps, by their ids; the tips for home; and the words to ask a chat for a task. They are read both ways, as every page's are (R159). An example's drawing and note are the only parts a page may leave out, and the reader tells whether a file holds a part without reading it. The labels the pages share are the site's dictionaries'. The names of a topic and of a trap are the widget's, so the page says them as the card does, and the build refuses a language of the site the widget does not speak. A page explains the three traps most frequent in the topic's reference tasks: each wrong option that names a trap counts once, at every level the topic is taught at, and ties go to the catalog's order. This is the count the service makes for a child new to a topic (R67), taken over the whole topic. The file holds words for exactly those three, and the build refuses one more or one less. An example's level, solver and answer are the site's data, `site/data.json`. Its solver is a program of its own, `site/solvers/<topic>/<name>.star`, written from the topic's template. `site/examples_test.go` runs every one of them in the sandbox the service runs a submitted solver in, with the service's limits and two runs under rotated labels, on its answer and four strangers, and holds it to the answer; a solver no example names fails the test. The build refuses an example of a page the catalog does not publish, an example at a level the topic is not taught at, and words that work through another number of examples than the data gives. The ask for a task gives the words to write in a chat — which a chat's model can honour, since `next_task` takes a topic of its choosing with a reason — and the way back to every topic. "Add to Claude" joins it with the home page's section on connecting (T66a), as the header's button does, since the home page does not yet say how to connect. A related topic leads to its page once published and says "soon" until then, as on the page of the topics. — *Why:* sixteen pages follow the pilot (T75.6–T75.9). A template with a closed set of blocks lets each of them be written as words and data alone. Counting, as the service already does, keeps the traps from being picked by taste. Naming them as the card does lets a parent who meets a trap in a chat find it on the page. A worked example states an answer that a parent passes on to a child, and fifty-one of them will be written by different hands, so each carries the proof the reference tasks carry (R52), run wherever an edit is. The draft's first two examples are reference tasks word for word — "we are both liars" is `kl-34-d2-1`, and the chain of three accusations is `kl-34-d3-5` — and a child could find a live task's answer in them, so the pilot's are variants of its own. — *Rejected:* a check run once and reported, which proves nothing about the next edit and leaves T75.6–T75.9 to build it again; an example's solver as lines replaced in a template and written into the data, where R52 keeps a program a program; traps chosen by hand; a component per topic; the topic's drawing and line shared with the page of the topics, which would have a page read another page's words, and whose drawing is sized for a card; the examples' facts in files of their own rather than the site's one file of data (R159); the test in `content/`, whose tests would then read the site's files. — *Consequence:* `site/` holds its first Go code, a test: it runs with `go test ./...`, and nothing is built from it. The anchors of the groups that a topic's path links, `/en/topics/#logic` and `/ru/topics/#logic` so far, join the published addresses (R158). That an example's words, in every language, state the task its solver searches and the answer it proves is held by the reader alone. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/site/TopicPage.tsx`, `TopicFoot.tsx`, `traps.ts`, `data.ts`, `pages.ts`, `render.tsx`, `reader.tsx`, `words.ts`, `style.css` and `locales/`; `site/content/<locale>/topics/`, `site/data.json`, `site/solvers/`, `site/examples_test.go`; `content/catalogs/topics.json`; `web/tools/sitecheck/published.ts`; SPEC 8.12.2; RUN.md (T75.5–T75.9, T66a).

**R177. No page of the site says it is a translation; the policy and the terms say which text holds, of themselves.** *(Decided by the author, 2026-10-04; it revises О-57 and О-50 on this point.)* The footer carries no note that a page is a translation and that the English version prevails — in Russian, «Это перевод. При расхождении действует английская версия.» — on any page, in any language. The rule that the English text prevails where a translation differs stays where it binds: the last section of the privacy policy and of the terms, "Languages", in every language the site carries. — *Why:* the author's decision. Which text holds matters only for the texts a parent agrees to, and those two say it in their own words; on the home page or a topic's page the line tells a parent nothing they need, and repeated under every page it reads as a disclaimer on a showcase. — *Rejected:* the note on every translated page, as О-57 had it; the note in the footer of the policy and the terms alone, which repeats their last section. — *Where it lives:* `site/content/<locale>/privacy.md` and `terms.md`, "Languages"; `web/src/site/Footer.tsx`, which has no such line, and its test in `web/src/site/render.test.tsx`; SPEC 8.12.2.

**R178. A card of a task on the site is drawn from where its lesson stands, and a card on a page names no build.** *(T75.10, 2026-10-04: approved by the author in the plan, which named the newest release on the card; the review found that release one behind, and a card on a page names none.)* *(Since R197 the home page's demo answers with every result the service would record for the lesson's task, each read at build time through the widget's own reader.)* *(Since R195 the site is published from the release, beside its delivery, and a card on a page names the release the site was built from; a site built from no release names none.)* *(Since R188 the home page draws its lesson's cards the same way: the task handed out, an option being checked, the hint open and the answer recorded.)* The page "Why" shows the widget's card of a task answered wrong. The widget's task card begins its lesson at a state it is given, by default the task just handed out with no answer; the seam's `StaticAnswer` (`web/src/site/StaticCard.tsx`) begins it at an answer recorded, so the component a chat draws after `submit_answer` is drawn at build time with no call made. A host passes no state, and the card behaves as before. The card's facts are the site's data — the topic, the grade, the five options, the drawing, the letter picked and the right one, the catalog's trap and the rating — and its words the page's: the child's name, the task, the trap's text and the solution. The widget's own readers of a task handed out and of an answer recorded read them, so a card the widget could not draw stops the build, and so does a card of a topic or a trap the catalog lacks, one in a grade its topic is not taught in, and one answered right or with "I don't know". A card on a page names no build: the seam tells the widget so (`NamesBuild`, which a host never sets), and the card's header draws no version. The site is published as soon as main moves, and the release that commit becomes is tagged only after the checks pass, so any version the site's build could name would be the release before the one the chats run. — *Why:* R170 drew the progress with the widget's code unchanged, because the progress screen takes all it shows from its payload. A task card keeps its lesson in its own state and reaches "answered" only through a call to the service, which a page drawn ahead cannot make. An optional starting state is the smallest change that lets the page draw the widget's own card rather than a copy, and a test holds the card on the page to the card a chat draws after the same answer: the same text, and the same elements in the same order, but for the build the chat's names. — *Rejected:* the card built from the widget's parts (`CardHeader`, `OptionList`, `TaskResult`), the copy R156 and R170 refuse; `WidgetApp` on a stand-in bridge with the option pressed, which needs a browser and a script at build time; the newest release at the site's build, which names the release before the one the chats run; the site rebuilt after every release, a second trigger for a label; the version hidden by the site's stylesheet, which leaves "dev" in the page for whatever reads it without the stylesheet — a reader mode, a text browser, a preview of the link. — *Consequence:* the home page's demo (T66a) can begin each scene's card at its state the same way. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/widget/TaskCard.tsx`, `web/src/site/StaticCard.tsx`, `web/src/site/why.ts`, `site/data.json` (`why.card`), `web/src/widget/version.ts` (`NamesBuild`), `web/src/widget/CardRoot.tsx`; SPEC 8.12.2.

**R179. "Why" says what research has found about olympiad maths, each finding under its work, and plainly what it does not prove.** *(Decided by the author, 2026-10-04: a section of its own after the first screen; the findings as their studies state them, with a plain note; these four works.)* The draft has no such section, and the author asked for one. It shows four findings, each as its work states it and linked by its DOI: Rebholz et al. (2022), a course of olympiad-style problems for grades 3 and 4 in Germany, quasi-experimental, its children picked by their teachers; Biondi, Vasconcellos and Menezes-Filho (2012), Brazil's olympiad for public schools and the national maths test, an observational study with statistical controls; Agarwal and Gaule (2020), scores at the International Mathematical Olympiad and later research careers; Nunes et al. (2007), the logical reasoning of six-year-olds and their maths, with a training study. A note under them says that most of this is observation — children who already enjoy maths come to olympiads more often — that it does not prove olympiad problems make a child more successful, and that MathTrail does not promise it. The page's claim about children's apps cites Radesky et al. (2022) rather than standing bare. The works are the site's data (`site/data.json`, `why.sources`): their authors, year, title, journal and DOI, which no language changes. A citation in a text is a slot the page fills with a link, as R159 has every link, in the form the site's dictionaries give it — "Rebholz et al., 2022", «Rebholz и др., 2022» — and the foot of the page lists every work it cites. The build refuses a finding of a work the data lacks, a work nothing on the page cites, a finding shown twice and a DOI that is no DOI. A link to a work is followed by the reader; nothing is loaded from another origin (R160). — *Why:* SPEC 8.12.2 has a claim with no source softened or given one, and nothing promised beyond what the program checks (PRODUCT 1). The literature read for the research programme (`research/literature/olympiad-and-measurement.md`, `problem-solving.md`) finds the effects of olympiad practice little studied, the evidence on olympians describing selected children, and no transfer to thinking outside mathematics; the page says the same in plain words, so that the showcase and the paper do not disagree. — *Rejected:* "children who solve olympiad problems are more successful" with no note: the studies show an association, and the one closest to MathTrail's ages selected its children; studies of olympiad winners at Russian universities, which speak of another age and would make the sources differ by language; a finding with no link to its work. — *Where it lives:* `site/data.json` (`why`), `site/content/<locale>/why.yaml` (`research`), `web/src/site/why.ts`, `web/src/site/WhyPage.tsx`, `web/src/site/locales/*.json` (`cite.*`); SPEC 8.12.2.

**R181. The site has a page of the techniques of problem solving, after a draft of its own; its solutions fold, and its examples are proved as a topic's are.** *(Since R183 the footer names the page "About" before the documents, as well as the documents.)* *(Decided by the author in the planning of T75.12, 2026-10-04: the solutions folded under "Show the solution", the coins on a round table replaced by a strip of nine cells, the address `/<locale>/techniques/`, and the second layer of the map on "Topics" renamed "Rules".)* On 2026-10-04 the author drew a sixth page, "Techniques" (`draft/ui/site/techniques.html`): eleven techniques of olympiad problem solving in three groups — to see a problem, to find the answer, to prove — each with what it is, when it helps, a problem, a drawing and a solution; Pólya's four steps before any technique; a hint on which technique the words of a problem suggest; and the way to the topics. It is published at `/<locale>/techniques/`, after "Topics" in the menu, and not in the footer, which names the documents (R167). One component, `web/src/site/TechniquesPage.tsx`, draws it from its words, `site/content/<locale>/techniques.yaml` (R159), and from the site's data, `site/data.json` under `techniques`: the groups in order, each with its techniques — a technique's name, which is its anchor on the page, the topics of the catalog it leads to, and its example's level, solver and answer — and the rows of the hint, each naming the techniques it suggests. A technique's solution — its steps, its answer and its drawing, which shows the answer too — folds under "Show the solution", a `<details>` that opens with no script, so that the problem can be tried first, as the page itself advises. The drawings are laid out by the page from the problems' numbers, in markup and classes, with no image and no script, and fit a phone's width (`web/src/site/TechniqueDrawings.tsx`); a drawing is hidden from a screen reader, since the steps say the same in words, and its caption is not. That a drawing shows the numbers its problem states and its solver proves is the reader's to hold, as the words' agreement with a solver is (R172): the drawing, the words and the solver each write the problem down, and no test reads all three. A topic a technique leads to links to its page once published and says "soon" until then, as a related topic does on a topic's page (R172). The number of techniques the first screen states is a plural wording of the site's dictionaries, since a page's text has no plural forms (R159). Every example's answer is proved as a topic's are: a solver of its own, `site/solvers/techniques/<name>.star`, written from a topic's template and run by `site/examples_test.go`; an example is set at a level of the product that one of its topics is taught at, or any topic, for a technique of none. The draft's problems become the product's: four were replaced because they nearly repeated live reference tasks — the two-digit numbers of 1, 2 and 3 (`enum-12-d4-1`), the twentieth bead of a pattern of three (`par-12-d4-1`), "I am a liar" (`kl-34-d1-1`) and thirteen pupils over twelve months (`pig-34-d1-5`); the grasshopper makes five jumps rather than seven, since the answer of `par-34-d3-4` is 7; the coins on a round table, whose places no solver can enumerate, became a strip of nine cells, where a search of all 512 positions finds the middle cell the one winning first move; the grades are the product's levels. The map on "Topics" calls its second layer "Rules" rather than "Techniques", which is now the page's name. — *Why:* the author's decisions. A page of techniques tells a parent what the topics' pages take for granted — how olympiad problems are attacked — and a folded solution keeps the page's own advice to try first. An answer a parent passes on is proved like every other on the site (R172), and no problem a child may meet live appears with its answer. — *Rejected:* the solutions open, as the draft draws them, which shows each answer beside its problem; the coins on the round table kept as a proof only the author checks, the one answer on the site no program would prove; the drawings as text drawings, as a topic's page has them, which the draft's diagrams outgrow; a drawing scrolled sideways inside its panel, which would need a focusable frame; the numbers "01" to "11" and the count of techniques typed into the words; the map's layer left "Techniques", a second meaning for the menu's word. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/site/TechniquesPage.tsx`, `TechniqueDrawings.tsx`, `Solution.tsx`, `techniques.ts`, `data.ts`, `pages.ts`, `frame.ts`, `style.css`, `locales/`; `site/content/<locale>/techniques.yaml`, `site/content/<locale>/topics.yaml` (`map.layers`); `site/data.json` (`techniques`), `site/solvers/techniques/`, `site/examples_test.go`; `web/tools/sitecheck/published.ts`; `draft/ui/site/techniques.html`; SPEC 8.12.2; RUN.md (T75.12).

**R183. The site has an "About" page — one family, each of them under a role — after the author's own draft, with no photos yet and the children named by their place in the family.** *(Decided by the author in the planning of T75.11, 2026-10-04: the words of the draft deleted on 2026-10-03, found again in a commit that was never pushed; the children as "Older son" and "Younger son", «Старший» and «Младший»; nothing in place of the photos, which a task of their own, T75.13, adds.)* *(Since R200 the page shows the family's photographs — the four of them together beside its first words, and each on their card — and its first words and the father's card follow the author's later draft.)* The page is `/<locale>/about/`, its words `about.yaml` (R159). It opens with "MathTrail is made by one family" and the four of them in a sentence; then a card for each — who they are in the family, the role they play, and what they do — under a heading a screen reader alone hears; then "Found a mistake, or have an idea?", which writes the contact address out in its sentence, as R167 has the footer do, links the issues of the code on GitHub, offers the address again as a button, and leads to the code. The address and the link to the code are the footer's, one constant each in `web/src/site/brand.ts`, and a test of the build holds every address on the page to the one the privacy policy names. The page closes the menu, as in the draft, and stands in the footer before the documents, under its own title, the same word as the menu's; "Research" will stand before it in both. The draft's photos and the children's names stay off the page, and nothing is drawn in place of the photos. A role's title is in the page's language: the draft set an English title over a Russian one on the Russian page, and the site holds the same words in every language (R159). — *Why:* the author's decisions. A parent who wonders who is behind the app finds out, and where to write, in the author's own words. A child's name on a public page is found by any search engine, and a place in the family is not. Photos raise questions of their own — the faces of children on a public page, the MIT licence that covers the repository's content, the metadata a photo file carries, its weight on the page (R160) — which T75.13 settles before any photo is published. — *Rejected:* the children's names, as the draft had them; a button saying "Write to us" with no address beside it, which leads nowhere without a mail program (R167); a "GitHub" button that leads to the issues, which would no longer be the footer's link — the sentence links them instead; an English title over a Russian one, which words held to one set of keys in every language cannot say on the English page without repeating it; initials in circles in place of the photos, the author's choice. — *Consequence:* R156 no longer holds in its "no About page", R167's footer names this page before the documents, and R181's footer "which names the documents" names this page too. A copy of the service rewrites `about.yaml` with its policy and its terms (`docs/self-hosting.md`). — *Where it lives:* `web/src/site/AboutPage.tsx`, `web/src/site/frame.ts`, `web/src/site/brand.ts`, `site/content/<locale>/about.yaml`, `web/src/site/locales/*.json` (`nav.about`), `web/tools/sitecheck/published.ts`, `web/scripts/prerender-site.test.ts`; SPEC 8.12.2; RUN.md (T75.11, T75.13).

**R188. The home page is ported in two steps, the first with no script: every step of a lesson stands beside a card of its own, its task is a variant of a reference task, and connecting is said as the README says it.** *(T66a.1, 2026-10-04: the author split T66a into T66a.1 and T66a.2, and chose in the plan to say the free plan and ChatGPT as the README and Claude's help say them.)* *(Since R197 the demo is live: the card on the first screen answers presses, the steps share one card on a wide window, and the address has a button that copies it; every wrong option has its trap in the page's words, and the data gives the rating after a right answer too.)* *(Since R202 the menu names neither of the home page's sections, and below 720 px the header leads to connecting through the logo, to the home page and its first screen.)* The home page is a page a component draws, `web/src/site/HomePage.tsx`, from its words in `site/content/<locale>/index.yaml`, as every other page is (R159). Its first screen gives the promise word for word as the README does, the catalog's grades, a button that leads to connecting and one that leads to a lesson, where it works today, and beside them the card of a task just handed out, in a frame of "your chat" under the parent's message. Then why asking the chat alone is not enough, in three pillars, the last naming three traps of the catalog as the card names them. Then how a lesson goes (`#lesson`): six steps, four numbered and two within the one before, each beside the widget's own card as it stands at that step — the task being written, the card a chat draws before the service has said anything of it; an option picked and being checked; the hint open; the wrong answer told, with its trap and the solution; the question the child asks in the chat once the answer is in, as messages of the chat under the card labelled as an illustration (R145); and the progress, its topics and its repeating mistakes open — and a note that the chat's log of tool calls shows each task with its answer (О-27). Then connecting (`#connect`): Claude's five steps, from Claude's help and the README, with the connector's address in full, which a click selects whole; ChatGPT as the README says it; and the accounts it takes. The page closes with the call to add MathTrail and the way to its code. The lesson's card is the site's data, `home.card`: a variant of the reference task `gaps-12-d5-4` — posts every 3 metres along a 12-metre fence, the right option C, 5 — with a trap of the catalog behind each wrong option (A `ignored_condition`, B `off_by_one`, D `wrong_operation`, E `number_from_text`), and a drawing of the fence's length and one gap, not of its posts, which would show the answer. A drawing on the site is numbers and lines alone, the same in every language, and `site/cards_test.go` holds every card's drawing to the service's own drawing check. The build refuses a wrong option with no trap behind it, a trap behind the right option, one the catalog does not have or one tied to no option, an example trap the catalog lacks, and every card "Why" refuses (`web/src/site/taskcard.ts`, shared by the two pages). The header's menu opens with the home page's two sections, and its one button, "Add to Claude", leads to connecting; below 720 px it is left out, and the menu leads there. Every button that asks to add MathTrail — on the home page, on "Why", at the end of a topic's page and of the techniques' — leads to `/<locale>/#connect`, and the two sections' anchors join the published addresses (R158). Every page names a picture for a shared link and says in words what it shows (`og:image`, `og:image:alt`, `twitter:card`), one per language, `site/assets/og-<locale>.png`, 1200 by 630: the home page of that language as it is built, laid out as a picture and photographed by `just site-og`. A language with no picture yet still builds, since a picture is photographed from a site already built, and the site's checker refuses to publish its pages: a picture a page names is a file the site serves. Where the product and the draft disagree, the product wins (R156): no field for a question under the card (R145); ranks, not ratings (R147); the catalog's traps, not the draft's; a drawing that does not give the answer; no time that nobody measured — not "two minutes to the first task", not "about a minute", and "Why" loses its "couple of minutes" too; the free plan said as the README and Claude's help say it, and as T64.4 found it — a lesson of fourteen tasks on its one custom connector; no switch of language inside the page, no licence line and no note of a translation (R142, R177); "Research" joins the menu with its page (T72.13). The demo is T66a.2: the first screen's card answering presses, one card for the steps that changes as they scroll by on a wide screen, and the button that copies the address — a script, which joins the build's list (R160), with its weight, its hydration and the focus of a card on a page to be settled in its plan. — *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-10-04. Without a script the page says all it has to say, and every card a step needs is already the widget's own (R178), so the page ships before the demo's open questions are settled rather than after them. The demo's task cannot be a reference task word for word, since a child could meet that task live and find its answer on the site (R172). The README is where the repository says how to connect, and the home page says it in the same words so the two cannot drift: a change to how a chat connects changes both. — *Rejected:* the draft's own words where they promise what the product does not do or nobody measured; the draft's drawing, `|--3--|--3--|--3--|--3--|`, which shows the five posts; a drawing that names its lengths in words of the page's language, which the service's drawing check refuses; one card for the whole lesson, which needs the script T66a.2 brings; a picture for sharing drawn apart from the page, which would drift from it. — *Consequence:* the home page weighs 171 KiB in English and 176 KiB in Russian with everything it can load (R160 counts every subset of the font), which leaves T66a.2 about 125 KiB for its script. A change to the home page's first screen deserves new pictures, photographed by `just site-og` and looked at before they are kept. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/site/HomePage.tsx`, `home.ts`, `taskcard.ts`, `Chat.tsx`, `StaticCard.tsx`, `frame.ts`, `Header.tsx`, `Layout.tsx`, `render.tsx`, `brand.ts`, `style.css` and `locales/`; `site/content/<locale>/index.yaml`, `site/data.json` (`home`), `site/assets/og-<locale>.png`, `site/cards_test.go`; `web/scripts/og.ts`, `web/scripts/prerender-site.ts`; `web/tools/sitecheck/published.ts` and `check.ts`; the justfile (`site-og`); SPEC 8.12.2; RUN.md (T66a.1, T66a.2).

**R195. The version in a card's header is a frame of two parts, the word in the card's language and the number without its "v", and the site is published from the release, its cards naming it.** *(Decided by the author, 2026-10-04, after their mockup 6c, "two parts: label and number"; the author chose the word over the number on a narrow card and the release on the site's cards. It revises R178's "a card on a page names no build".)* The header MathTrail opens a card with closes its line with a frame of two parts: the word for a version, `app.version` in the card's language — "version", "версия", machine-translated in twenty of the 22 dictionaries under R140 — quiet on the `--hover` ground, and the number in `--font-mono` on the card's `--surface`, the build's version without the "v" a release's tag begins with (`versionNumber`): `0.2.5`, `0.2.5-4-gfcd5198-dirty` between releases, `dev` for a build told nothing. On a narrow card the word stands over the number, as the name stands over the grade; on a wide card they stand side by side, as the mockup draws them. The frame keeps its width, so that no word in it is broken: where there is no room, on a card of 320 px in Russian or Japanese, the grade wraps instead. It takes at most 30% of a narrow line and 40% of a wide one, a long number wrapping inside its part, which leaves MathTrail's name its room in the pseudo-language too. The line between the parts is the frame's colour seen through a 1 px gap, and the frame clips the parts to its corners (`overflow: hidden`, which every browser a host runs on knows and which squeezes nothing, since the frame never shrinks), so neither part has a side of its own: the word runs the way the card's language does, the number is `dir="ltr"`, and a side named on either would land on the wrong edge of a card written right to left. The frame stays hidden from a screen reader (R154), and a space between the parts keeps them two words without the stylesheet. The tag, the handshake's version, `/health` and the log keep the "v". The site is published from the release: `release.yml` calls `pages.yml` with the release's tag beside `deploy.yml`, on the same condition that main still ends at the commit; the site is built from the tag with `VITE_VERSION` set to it, and its seam names the build only when the build was told one (`versionGiven`), so the task cards on the home page and on "Why" name the release, and a site built on a pull request, by hand or on a laptop names none rather than `dev`. A push to main no longer publishes the site; a run started by hand publishes its branch as it stands and names no release, which is how a new deployment puts its privacy policy online before its first release. — *Why:* the author's mockup and choices of 2026-10-04. A site published on the push could name only the release before the one the chats run (R178); built from the release, it names the one they run. Beside the delivery rather than after it: the site is out in about a minute and the service rolled out in about three, so the privacy policy and the topics' pages a card links to are online before the service that points at them, as `docs/self-hosting.md` asks, and neither holds the other back when it fails. Measured in Chromium in every language at 320, 360, 428 and 640 px: side by side, the frame and the grade fit on no card of 320 px and on no Russian or Japanese one of 360; with the word over the number, the grade keeps its line in every language from 360 px up. — *Rejected:* the site published after the delivery, which leaves the service ahead of its policy and its topics' pages for the minute the site takes, and for good when the site fails; the push kept beside the release's call, a second trigger for a label (R178); a version asked for on the run started by hand, which could publish an older release's site with its older policy; the frame side by side on a narrow card, which wraps the grade on every phone of 320 px and on Russian ones of 360; the frame left to shrink, which broke "версия" in two on a card of 320 px; `overflow: clip`, which Safari learned only in 16 and which leaves the parts' corners sticking out of the frame before it; sides and radii on the parts; the "v" kept, which the word beside the number already says. — *Consequence:* the site goes live 10 to 25 minutes after a merge rather than one, after the checks, the release and its own build: a fix to the site waits for green checks, and a main that fails them holds the site back with the service. A site that failed turns "Release and deploy" red, and is started again with "Re-run failed jobs", as a delivery is. The cards on the site name a release a little before the chats run it and, when a delivery fails, until the next one succeeds. The first card of the home page is 238 px wide on a phone of 320, narrower than any chat draws it, and there the grade wraps onto a second line in English and a third in Russian. The site's pictures for a shared link, photographed from a site built on a laptop, name no release. The README's pictures of a task and of a wrong answer show the word over the number. RUN.md's research tasks that plan to publish through Pages on every push to main (T72.12, T72.13, T73.2, T73.4) publish through the release instead. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/widget/version.ts` (`versionNumber`, `versionGiven`), `web/src/widget/CardRoot.tsx`, `web/src/design/thread.tsx`, `web/src/design/mathtrail.css`, `web/locales/` (`app.version`), `web/src/site/StaticCard.tsx`; `.github/workflows/pages.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`; `docs/screens/`; SPEC 8.12; `docs/self-hosting.md`; RUN.md (T79).

**R197. The home page's demo brings its cards alive with one script: the card on the first screen answers presses, the steps of a lesson share one card on a wide window, and the connector's address gets a button that copies it; the script carries none of the widget's dictionaries, no zod and no host's library, and no card on the site leads to the progress.** *(T66a.2, 2026-10-04: the author chose in the plan the cards the steps already have for the shared card, a card on the site with no way to the progress, and "Another task" answered by the card a task is written on and then the same task.)* *(Since 2026-10-05 the card has no "I don't know", and the demo carries a result for each option alone; `choices.ts` holds the letters and `dontKnow`: R208.)* The demo is one module, `web/src/demo/`, which the site's build makes into `assets/demo.js` beside its stylesheets: a third input of `vite.config.site.ts`, under a fixed name. The home page names it itself, `<script type="module">`, and carries all it needs in a block of JSON a browser never runs, `<script type="application/json" data-demo>`, every "<" in it escaped: the page's language, the widget's words in that language alone, the lesson's task as a card is handed it, and what the service would record of every answer — each option and "I don't know" — read at build time through the widget's own reader (`homeResultsOf`). Each wrong option's trap is said in the page's words, `task.traps.<letter>`, and the site's data gives the rating after a right answer as well as after a wrong one (`home.card.rating`: `before`, `wrong`, `right`); the rating moves on correctness alone, so "I don't know" moves it as a wrong answer does, and the build refuses a right answer that does not raise it or a wrong one that does not lower it. The card on the first screen is the widget's own `TaskCard`, put in place of the still card and drawn there in one step, which the page is not painted in the middle of, so that it measures the room it has, and a card that cannot be drawn gives the still one its place back: no hydration, which would keep the still card's attributes and its `inert`. It answers presses through a stand-in for the service: an answer is recorded after 1.1 s as the data says, with the hint as the card says it was used, and the rating moved on from where the answer before left it. "Another task" is the child's message in the page's chat frame, and under it the card a task is written on, `ComingCard`, which the stand-in tells the task is being written and then hands the lesson's task again, under an id of its own; the chat's frame takes the focus when the card that asked gives way. Nothing is sent and nothing kept: the stand-in for the host calls no tool, opens no page and tells no model, and the card shows no link. On a window of 960 px and wider the cards the build drew for the steps are moved, never copied, into one column beside the steps that stays in sight; the card of the step at the middle of the window is shown, and the one giving way fades, at once under `prefers-reduced-motion`. The chat's frame there is shorter than a tall card, which the script aligns in it — a question at the bottom, an answer's replies 180 px from the top — and the step at the middle is found again, as the cards are staged, once the page's own typeface has come, and in the next frame after the window changes size. The cards stay inert, and the column is hidden from a screen reader, which reads the steps, whose words say what each card shows: read after every step, the shown card's chat would be cut off from the step it belongs to. On a narrower window every step keeps its own card and the page is as built. The button that copies the address waits in a `<template>` beside it; the script puts it in the address's pill only where the browser can write to the clipboard, says "Copied" for 1.6 s and announces it politely, and selects the whole address when the copy is refused. Its words are the site's dictionaries' (`copy.action`, `copy.done`). The widget changes only by five seams, each with a test of its own, and a card in a chat behaves as before. Ids: Preact numbers the ids of every root from the start, so a live card would take the ids of the cards drawn at build time; `useScopedId` puts the scope of `IdScope` (`web/src/design/ids.ts`) before them, the live card's beginning with "live-", and a rule of Biome keeps `useId` out of the card's code. Words: `words.ts` loads no dictionary; `web/src/widget/dictionaries.ts` holds the twenty-two, with `cardWords`, `lessonLanguages` and `catalogSkills`; `WordsContext` has no words of its own, and a name is asked of the card's words (`Words.has`), never of an English dictionary the card was not given. Service: a card asks a `Service` from its context — record an answer, say how a task stands, read the progress, save a change to the profile — which in a chat is `serviceThrough(host)`, the tools called and their replies read with zod (`hostedService.ts`); `letters`, `dontKnow` and every choice live in `choices.ts`; and `edits.ts` is gone. Progress: `CardFrame` opens the progress over a card only where the place the card is drawn in gives `OpensProgress`; the widget's page gives `ProgressOverCard`, and without it the top line is `NameBar`, the child's name with no button. The site's still cards and its live one give none, since a page keeps no profile, as the choice of the topic already found (R194); StaticCard's test holds a site card to a chat's but for that line. Focus: a card takes back a focus it has lost only where it is the whole document, as in a chat's frame (`CardIsTheDocument`, `focus.ts`); the live card is a part of the page, so a focus on nothing stays where the reader left it — on the page, or in a browser that gives a pressed button no focus — and from a button gone the browser takes the next step itself. `aria-live` needs no seam: the live regions of the first screen's card speak only after the reader's own press, and the shared column's cards are inert. The script weighs 53.2 KB, and the home page comes to 267,498 bytes in English and 281,904 in Russian with everything it can load (261.2 and 275.3 KiB) of the 300 KiB R160 allows; the page's dictionary is 17 and 25 KB of that. The site's checker gains a rule, `script`: a script the site serves loads no other file, since the checker weighs a script whole and follows no import, and names none of the usual ways to send something or keep it in the reader's browser — `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `sendBeacon`, `WebSocket`, `EventSource`, `new Image`, `serviceWorker`, `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, `indexedDB`, `cookie` — each found as a word, however the script reaches it. A script could still hide one, and a reader of its source is the last check. The build's test holds `demo.js` to carrying no dictionary of the widget, no zod and nothing of a host's library. The sharing pictures are photographed with JavaScript off, the page as it is built. — *Why:* the task, and the author's choices of 2026-10-04. The cards the steps already have are the widget's own, drawn from where each step's lesson stands (R178), so the shared card needs no code of the widget and no weight; one live card for the steps would need the progress screen in the script. A card on the site with a working way to the progress would carry the progress and the profile's form, with a form that saves nothing, and both would take the Russian page past 300 KiB; a way that vanished only from the live card would vanish under the reader's eyes as the script loads. The task asked for the page's language and English; English is left out, since a test holds every dictionary to the English keys, so it falls back to nothing, and it would have taken the Russian page to about 299 KiB. — *Rejected:* one live card for the steps, its scenes set by a stand-in for the host; keeping "Profile & progress" working on the live card; hiding it on the live card alone; hydrating the still cards; a second variant of the task for "Another task", which is a text more to read; English beside the page's words; an `IntersectionObserver` for the step at the middle, which the tests' browser has none of that fires; the widget's dictionary cut to the keys the demo shows, which would save 10 to 15 KB at the risk of a word shown as its key; the copy button drawn hidden in the page, which `.s-btn`'s display shows anyway and a reader without styles shows too; a new icon for copying. — *Consequence:* every card of a task on the site, on the home page and on "Why", and the card of a task being written, has a top line that names the child and leads nowhere, and the sharing pictures are photographed anew; the README's pictures, of cards in a chat, are unchanged. T66b will take the home page past 300 KiB whatever the demo saves, through the other languages' font subsets and their dictionaries — Bengali, Thai and Hindi are about 31 KB each — so the limit, or the demo's words per language, is a decision of its own there. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/demo/` (`main.tsx`, `data.tsx`, `service.ts`, `hero.tsx`, `stage.ts`, `copy.ts`, `testing/home.ts`); `web/src/widget/` (`dictionaries.ts`, `words.ts`, `names.ts`, `choices.ts`, `service.ts`, `hostedService.ts`, `focus.ts`, `CardFrame.tsx`, `ProgressOverCard.tsx`, `TaskCard.tsx`, `ComingCard.tsx`, `TopicChoice.tsx`, `ProfileScreen.tsx`, `ProfileForm.tsx`, `WidgetApp.tsx`); `web/src/design/` (`ids.ts`, `thread.tsx`, `mathtrail.css`); `web/src/i18n/words.ts`; `web/src/site/` (`HomePage.tsx`, `home.ts`, `taskcard.ts`, `StaticCard.tsx`, `Layout.tsx`, `style.css`, `locales/`); `site/content/<locale>/index.yaml` (`task.traps`), `site/data.json` (`home.card.rating`); `web/vite.config.site.ts`; `web/scripts/prerender-site.ts`, `og.ts`; `web/tools/sitecheck/check.ts` (`script`); `web/biome.json`; SPEC 8.12; RUN.md (T66a.2).

**R198. The site has a page of the coach, a product of its own still in the making, and the page frames the coach's prototype, the export of the tool it was drawn in with two small pieces taken out of it; a document a page frames is weighed apart from the page.** *(Decided by the author, 2026-10-04, after their draft `draft/ui/couch/MathTrail - Тренер.html`, removed once ported; carried out in T75.14. It narrows to every other page T75.11's rule that the site neither links to the author's paid product nor mentions it, and it revises R160's weight and R167's menu.)* *(On 2026-10-05 the author had the second of the two pieces taken out: an unused note of the designer's that named a contest.)* *(Since R202 the page of the coach opens the menu, the home page's sections gone from it, and the menu folds below 992 px.)* *(Since R209 the menu folds below 1100 px, "Research" standing in it before "About".)* The coach is a product of its own that the author is making: a tablet app for children of 7 to 12, in which a companion the child chooses leads through a problem in Pólya's four steps on a canvas the child draws and writes on, and never gives the answer away. It will call MathTrail's service for olympiad tasks, which costs the author nothing, and the Gemini API, which the author pays for, so it will have a paid plan beside a free one. The service is not changed by it, and still calls no model of its own. The site shows the coach on a page of its own, `/<locale>/coach/`, the third entry of the menu, right after connecting, as the draft has it, under "Coach" and «Тренер»; the footer does not name it. The page is the site's own, drawn by its components and read in full without a script (R160): a badge that says the coach is in development, the heading and what the coach does, a button to the prototype further down and one to the techniques, counted from the site's data as the page of the techniques counts them; three cards, on Pólya's four steps, a canvas like a notebook and a companion of the child's own; the prototype in its frame; and what the prototype is, and what it only pretends to do yet: recognising handwriting and drawings, the companion's voice, problems from a photo. Its Russian words are the draft's, but for three of its dashes, which give way to colons and a clause so that no sentence is torn; the English are written from them and are the source (R159). The page is in every language of the site (R158). The prototype speaks English alone, in all of them, and is never translated, T66b included. The prototype is the document the draft's frame held, as the design tool exported it: the draft's manifest entry `2b535897-8550-4a66-a57f-c30023884ead`, unpacked, 1,852,240 bytes, SHA-256 `e5cdfb4f6bb5afe457143f8acc3d147b886eede69eb48078b4251603b9321f34`, less two small pieces of its page: the tag `<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com">`, which would open a connection to Google from every visit, as the privacy policy promises the site does not, and one entry of the designer's notes, which the prototype does not use and which named a contest, taken out at the author's word on 2026-10-05. It ships byte for byte as `site/assets/coach-prototype.html`, 1,851,793 bytes, SHA-256 `cd07c9658db2f82fb6103c47e327b9cf27ef3f87a5904d46b3f6278a4bd0666d`, at `/assets/coach-prototype.html`. Its plan stays, by the author's decision, with the price in the screen for grown-ups: Free, and Plus at $10 a month with an "Upgrade" button that does nothing. It unpacks itself in the browser from data it carries: copies of React and ReactDOM 18.3.1, the design tool's runtime, six pictures of the companions and the fonts Noto Sans and Noto Serif 2.015, through inline scripts, `blob:` addresses and `DecompressionStream`. It makes no request of its own, keeps nothing, and fits its tablet, 1272 by 932 px, to the width it is given and never wider. The page frames it lazily (`loading="lazy"`), under a title a screen reader says, in a frame of the tablet's proportions and 2 px more, so that a narrow screen shows no empty band under it and the last row its fit rounds up to brings no scroll bar into the frame; a browser with no container units keeps the proportions by the frame's aspect ratio. The section is 1320 px wide, as in the draft, on paper of the prototype's own colour (`--s-paper`), and a link under it opens the prototype alone in a tab of its own. The fonts' licence travels beside it as `assets/noto-license.txt`, the OFL text of the fonts' repository at their tag. The copies it carries have a section of their own in `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES`; CodeQL and Sonar leave it out, as they leave the drafts; Git keeps its bytes as they are (`-text`) and shows no diff of it, and `robots.txt` keeps crawlers from reading it, since read alone it would make a search result with no word of what it is; its address may still be listed from the links to it. A document a page frames is weighed apart from the page: the page keeps its 300 KiB without what it frames, and the framed document, with everything it loads, has 2 MiB of its own (`--max-frame-bytes`). It is counted once however many pages frame it, and held to the rules of links and of origins a page is held to; a page that another frames is weighed as a page, and a framed file that is no HTML is weighed by its size, nothing read in it. The checker reads markup alone, and the prototype's addresses are in its data, much of it compressed, so the build's test unpacks every text the prototype carries and holds it to the site's origin: every absolute address in them is a namespace of XML, the page a message of React's errors points to, the Babel its runtime fetches for a component imported by address, or the key of a copy it carries; every such copy is in it; and it imports no component, by address or by name. Without these its runtime would fetch React or Babel from another origin, or a component's file from the site. The build's test counts as a page every HTML file but a framed document, lists the framed documents, and holds the menu to open with the home page's two sections and the coach after them. In Russian the menu's seven entries no longer fit in a row beside the mark and the tools at 1100 px, so it folds behind its button below 1200 px rather than 1100. Measured at the site's 14 px of padding, in Chromium with a classic scroll bar, the entries stay in a row down to 1180 px in Russian and 1096 in English; in WebKit, down to 1166 and 1082. Measured too, in Chromium and WebKit at 320, 360, 640 and 1280 px in both languages: no page scrolls sideways, the frame is of the tablet's proportions and its document does not scroll, the prototype unpacks with no error, and neither the page, nor the frame, nor the prototype opened alone asks anything of another origin. — *Why:* the author's request of 2026-10-04, to show the future product on the site and touch its prototype as little as possible. The page around the prototype is drawn anew rather than shipped as drawn: the draft's page is a self-unpacking bundle that shows nothing without a script and copies the site's header and footer with links to the draft's own files. A frame keeps the prototype's scripts, styles and fonts out of the page, and the page out of them. The weight rule held a frame to the page's budget, which a whole application cannot meet; a budget of its own keeps a frame from growing unnoticed, as the page's keeps the page. The price stays because the coach will cost the author an API to run, which a free plan alone cannot pay for. — *Rejected:* the draft's page shipped as it is, which reads as nothing without a script and leads its menu to files the site does not have; a picture of the prototype with a button that opens it, which keeps the page's budget without an exception but lets a reader try the prototype only in a tab of its own; the frame's document added to the page's weight under a budget raised for every page, which would let every page grow to 2 MiB unnoticed; the prototype with its price or its plan taken out; the version the author published as a claude.ai artifact, which is the design tool's canvas, with a panel to jump between screens and without the companions' pictures; the draft's 10 px of padding in the menu, which the fold at 1200 px does not need; a `sandbox` on the frame, which the prototype's serving from the site's own origin, where nothing is kept, would not make safer, and which the link that opens it alone would not carry anyway. — *Consequence:* the site names the author's paid product on this page alone. T75.11's "no link to the author's paid product, no mention of it" holds for every other page, and the free app itself, its tool results, its instructions and its card, still carries no link to it. The free card calls itself an olympiad coach and leads to the site's topics (R186), whose menu now leads to the page of another product, with a price inside its prototype; T71.1 checks this against the directories' rules on advertising. The pages that say MathTrail is free, with no subscriptions, such as "Why", the terms and the README, speak of the free app and are not changed here. On a phone the tablet shows at about a fifth of its size, and the link opens it alone. The page and its prototype weigh about 1.26 MB over the wire, compressed, and the frame loads once a reader comes near it, which on a desktop is about as soon as the page opens. The prototype's text keeps the designer's other notes, which it does not use, in Russian, and the design tool's runtime names no licence; both stay as exported, open questions for the author. An eighth entry of the menu, "Research" (T72.13), fits in a row in Russian at no width the site has, and T72.13 decides how the menu holds it. A new export of the prototype replaces the file and its hash, which the build's test holds the prototype to, so that no export ships unread: the test refuses it until its `preconnect` comes out again, and the note that named a contest comes out of it again too, which only a reader checks (R22). A fork removes the page and the prototype (`docs/self-hosting.md`). — *Where it lives:* `site/assets/coach-prototype.html`, `site/assets/noto-license.txt`, `site/content/{en,ru}/coach.yaml`, `web/src/site/CoachPage.tsx`, `web/src/site/pages.ts`, `web/src/site/frame.ts`, `web/src/site/brand.ts` (`coachPrototypePath`), `web/src/site/locales/` (`nav.coach`), `web/src/site/style.css`, `web/src/site/metadata.ts` (`robots`), `web/src/site/techniques.ts` (`allTechniques`), `web/scripts/prerender-site.ts`, `web/scripts/prerender-site.test.ts` (`framed`), `web/tools/sitecheck/` (`maxFrameBytes`, `frameWeight`, `published.ts`), `justfile` (`_license-list`), `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES`, `.gitattributes`, `.github/workflows/codeql.yml`, `sonar-project.properties`; PRODUCT 8 (О-57); SPEC 8.12.2; RUN.md (T75.14, T66b, T71.1); `docs/self-hosting.md`.

**R200. The page "About" shows the family's photographs — the four of them together beside its first words, and each on their card — kept out of image search, weighed apart from the page and not under the MIT licence.** *(Decided by the author in the planning of T75.13, 2026-10-04: the photographs and the layout of the author's later draft, which the author keeps outside the repository with the originals; the children's faces shown, and the files closed to crawlers; the younger son's portrait from the draft rather than the one beside it with English captions; the draft's new first words and father's card; and the photographs left out of a page's weight, a photograph being a case of its own.)* There are five: `family`, 3:4 at 900 by 1200 pixels, on a panel beside the first words, and four portraits, 4:5 at 960 by 1200 — `dad`, `mum`, `older-son`, `younger-son` — on the cards, twice the widest the page shows them and as much as the originals give. Those names are the ones the family's words go by on the page, `team.members.<name>`, so a card cannot set one of them over another's words, and none of them is a child's own name; the order of the cards is the list in `web/src/site/brand.ts`. Each photograph is `<img src width height alt>`, with no `srcset`: one file each, its size stated so that nothing jumps as it arrives, the portraits loaded as the reader nears them, and an `alt` that is a word of the page's file in its language, saying what the photograph shows with no name and no age. The files are WebP in `site/assets/photos/<name>.webp`, made by `just site-photos <dir>` from originals that stay outside the repository: each is cropped to its proportions with its top kept, where the faces are, scaled to its size, and written by Chromium's own encoder from a canvas with no transparency, of which only the image chunk is kept — no EXIF, no XMP, no colour profile. A test holds every kept file to WebP of one image chunk at the size `brand.ts` states, and the build copies the files as they are. `robots.txt` closes `/assets/photos/` to crawlers: the page is found by a search, its photographs not apart from it, by every crawler that heeds the file. The site's checker weighs a page without the files below its photo directory, `/assets/photos/`, which the build's test holds to the site's own; any other picture still counts in full. The README says that the MIT License does not cover the photographs, which are the family's own, and a copy of the service puts its own in their place or takes them off the page (`docs/self-hosting.md`). A role's title stays one, in the page's language (R183). — *Why:* the author's decisions. A parent who wonders who is behind the app sees the family, as the draft shows it. Five photographs squeezed into what 300 KiB leaves beside the page would blur, and the page "About" is the one page that shows any; the limit is kept for what grows unnoticed — styles, fonts, scripts. A child's face on a public page is found by every search engine that indexes images, and closing the files keeps the faces to the page. A canvas carries the picture alone, so no place, time or camera leaves with it by mistake. — *Rejected:* one photograph, or the portraits without the family together, where the draft has both; `srcset` with several sizes, which the checker reads nothing of and would have to learn to weigh; a budget of a photograph's own, by the author's decision: the test of the kept files holds a photograph to the tool's format and to the size the page states, and its weight is let be; weighing every `<img>` apart from the page, which would let any heavy picture in unnoticed; the younger son's portrait with English captions; the originals in the repository, some 9 MB the family keeps. — *Consequence:* R160 weighs a page without the site's photographs; R183's "no photos yet" no longer holds; the draft and the originals, some 20 MB, stay out of the repository's history: a snapshot of the tree took them in on 2026-10-05, and the branch was rebuilt without them. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/site/brand.ts`, `web/src/site/AboutPage.tsx`, `site/content/<locale>/about.yaml`, `site/assets/photos/`, `web/scripts/photos.ts`, the `justfile` (`site-photos`), `web/scripts/prerender-site.ts`, `web/src/site/metadata.ts`, `web/tools/sitecheck/`; README; `docs/self-hosting.md`; SPEC 8.12.2; RUN.md (T75.13).

**R202. The site's menu names pages alone: "How it works" and "Connect", the home page's sections, leave it, and it folds below 992 px.** *(Decided by the author on 2026-10-05.)* *(Since R209 the menu has six entries, "Research" before "About", and folds below 1100 px, where they still fit in Russian.)* The menu is "Coach", "Why", "Topics", "Techniques" and "About", and "Research" when its page comes. The home page leads to its sections itself, its first screen to connecting and to a lesson, and the header's one button, "Add to Claude", still leads to connecting from every page at 720 px and wider. Below 720 px the button is left out, as before, and the header leads to connecting only through the logo, to the home page, whose first screen has the button; the pages on why, on the techniques and on each topic end with one of their own. The words `nav.lesson` and `nav.connect` leave the site's dictionaries. With five entries the menu fits in a row beside the logo and the tools down to 930 px in Russian, the longer of the two languages — measured at the site's 14 px of padding in Chromium with a classic scroll bar, and to 920 px in WebKit — so it folds below 992 px, a width the stylesheet already keeps for the pages' first screens, rather than below 1200 px; an entry more wants the width measured again. — *Why:* the author's choice: a menu of the site's pages, with the header's button for connecting. The fold follows R198's rule, which puts it where the entries stop fitting, and its margin of some sixty pixels covers the way another browser sets the same font. — *Rejected:* the header's button kept below 720 px, where the logo, the languages and the menu's button already fill a phone 320 px wide; connecting added to the folded menu as an entry for phones alone, a second way to the same section that the home page's first screen gives a press away; the fold left at 1200 px, which would hide behind the button, between 992 and 1200 px, a menu that fits. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/site/frame.ts`, `web/src/site/locales/`, `web/src/site/style.css`, `web/scripts/prerender-site.test.ts`; SPEC 8.12.2.

**R204. The page "Research" takes every number from one file made at build time: the bench's numbers computed by CI from the commit it builds, the product's from its code and content, the paper's from the PDF, and the live numbers later.** *(T72.12, decided with the author on 2026-10-05; the design is `docs/research-page.md`.)* *(T72.13.1, 2026-10-05: the eleven cells are a command of the bench, `page`, rather than a set of rules, since the bench's own run reads comparisons they lack, and a second command, `page-file`, adds what the commit being built adds. Each rule's number carries its own mark. The cache is keyed by the bench's program as built, which holds every input the cells depend on, and by whether the processor has FMA, which `math.Exp` reads as it runs, rather than by a list of the sources and the image's tag. The product exports the corridor's bounds and the list of its checks, and the shift is read from the solver's own relabelling. The PDF is served under `/assets/`. The design says so in its §4–§7 and §10.)* *(T72.13.2, 2026-10-05: the site's build reads the file from `--research`, made first by `just site`, and copies the PDF from beside it, held to its size and its hash. An interval is written as its two ends, since `formatRange` writes one whose ends round alike as a single approximate number. A book is named by its title, and its author with the year they died. The menu folds below 1100 px (R209). The thesis on the sealed answer stands as the draft has it, since R152 had already taken the answer out of the card's memory; the design says so in its §3 and §12.)* *(Since R221 the live block's counts are by month and range, every instructions version together, and a range of fewer than ten children or thirty answers is left out rather than folded into `(others)`.)* The page, `/<locale>/research/`, ports the author's draft, `research/draft-ui/MathTrail - Исследование.html`, section by section in the site's own look: the paper's title and its PDF, four theses, the student model against its goals (`#student-model`), the live numbers (`#live`), where the reference tasks came from (`#sources`) and the whole paper (`#paper`). It reads in full without a script and draws its charts at build time in SVG and HTML, each beside a table, in Onest alone.
- **One file, `research.json`.** It is made at build time, not committed. Its blocks:
  - `bench`: eleven cells run by a new command of the bench, `page` — the service and the rule before R187 on G0, G0-topics1, G2-half and G3, the oracle on G0, G2-half and G3 — read by a `pageCriterion` that holds the goals of the step and of mastery against the service;
  - `product`: the counts and constants the page quotes — the checks through an exported list, the corridor's bounds exported, and the shift read from the solver's own relabelling;
  - `paper`: the clean PDF's facts;
  - `live`: `coming` until T72.15.

  It holds every number in full, rounded only on the page, and each goal's value as the criterion reads it, the lag as a size. It is the same byte for byte for the same inputs on amd64, where CI runs, and T73.2 later writes it from the research pipeline unchanged.
- **CI computes the bench's numbers** in the site's build job, cached by a hash of every input the cells depend on. A pull request shows them in the job's summary. A release computes them from its own commit, with nothing restored, and publishes nothing when they fail.
- **Goals are bounded against the current service**, as the criterion bounds them. Where a bound is computed from the service's own value of the same measure — the lag, the corridor of a child who learns, the answers until a mastery — its row says "baseline" rather than a mark. The screen's goals for answers 6–20, which could only ever say that, are left out.
- **The paper's title is on the page from the first release**, though the paper is under double-blind review. The AIED 2026 call accepted preprints that follow Springer's policies; the 2027 call was not out on 2026-10-02 and is read again when it is.
- **The PDF is the named build**, linked from every language until T73.3 builds the Russian one. It appears only once it prints no `[Author]`, `[Affiliation]` or `\TBD` — today the three of K05 and the artifact's DOI of K11. Search engines may index it.
- **No number is typed by hand.**
  - A number from data is written as `<data value>`, and a number the text chooses is marked as given.
  - An identifier from data with digits in it is written in its own element: a commit in `<code>`, a date in `<time>`.
  - A test of the rendered page in every language fails on any other digit. The words hold none.
- **The live block shows its frame** and says the numbers are still to come. T72.15 adds monthly public aggregates to T67.2's tables, under R192's rules:
  - ten children and thirty answers behind a shown cell, ten children rather than T72.12's ten answers;
  - the groups that fall short folded into `(others)`, so no hidden group can be had by subtraction;
  - counts rounded to five;
  - each closed month on its own.
- **The guard holds the model** on every pull request, and a release follows only a green CI. The page's own numbers, read off 1,000 children, are not held to the guard's bands, which are drawn for 300.

— *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-10-05.
- A committed run cannot name the commit it was computed on, would need the whole bench rerun and a ten-thousand-line diff for any change of the model's inputs, and parts in its last digits on arm64. A run computed from the commit has none of these, and is what T73.2 does anyway.
- A cell depends on its rule and its generator alone, so eleven cells give the whole run's numbers to the last digit, in about 3.4 processor-minutes.
- A bound computed from the service's own value of the same measure decides the service's mark in advance: a share of it is never reached and a multiple of it always is, so a mark there would say nothing.
- The paper was the PDF of the draft: the author chose to show it at once rather than wait for the venue, and to show the named build only once it is clean.

— *Rejected:*
- the bench's numbers committed and checked for freshness;
- the draft as it is, which needs a script and loads from elsewhere;
- Source Serif 4;
- a Looker Studio report in a frame;
- Observable Plot 0.6.17, which needs a virtual DOM and writes its own tick labels;
- Vega 6.4.0 and Vega-Lite 6.4.3, which measure text only with node-canvas and format numbers outside `Intl`;
- Chart.js 4.5.1 and uPlot 1.6.32, which draw on a canvas;
- the anonymous PDF on the product's own site;
- the named PDF with its placeholders;
- the draft's "last 30 days";
- shares behind ten answers;
