- the page's own numbers held to the guard's bands, drawn for 300 children;
- the report's exact numbers.

— *Consequence:*
- T72.13 builds the page and likely splits in two under rule 12, its data and the page.
- T72.15 is a new task for the live numbers.
- T73.3 and T73.4 publish the paper by this decision rather than ask again.
- A fork removes the paper's PDF and title (`docs/self-hosting.md`).
- The page's thesis on the sealed answer says what holds today: the card the service draws holds no answer, since `next_task` draws it and `read_task` carries none (R152), which already answers the research's K10. What the seal does not reach is the chat itself, the model that wrote the task and the host's record of the tools called (О-27).

— *Where it lives:* `docs/research-page.md`; SPEC 8.12.2; RUN.md (T72.12, T72.13, T72.15).

**R206. The bare domain sends the reader on to the English front page at once, and the language is switched there.** *(Decided by the author on 2026-10-05; it revises R24, whose apex named the product and handed the reader its languages, and the same words in R158.)* `/` is a page that refreshes to `/en/` with no delay and no script. It loads no stylesheet, no font and no picture — its icon, which a browser fetches aside, keeps nothing waiting — and holds one link to `/en/` for a browser that holds the refresh back. Its head is the English front page's — the title, the one line and the picture a shared link shows — so that a chat or a directory that previews the bare domain without following the refresh still shows the product; its canonical and `og:url` name `/en/`. It names no translations and is left out of the sitemap. The x-default of every page, a front page's included, is the reference locale's version. The site's checker reads a refresh as a browser does — one that waits first, or that a browser would not act on, is no redirect, and its page is judged as any other — and lets the apex alone send its reader on: to a page of the site, whose address is then its canonical, with no translations of its own to name. A page of a language that sends its reader on is a translation the language has lost. `/` stays on the list of published addresses, since a listing holds it. — *Why:* the author opened the bare domain expecting the home page and met a doorway; a reader should land on the product, and the switch in the header is all a reader of another language needs. The site's host answers no redirect of its own, and a refresh with no delay is the redirect a static site has; Google reads one as permanent. A refresh comes due only once the page has loaded: a page with a stylesheet and fonts would first show itself, which is the doorway the author did not want to see, while a page that loads nothing is gone as soon as it is read. — *Rejected:* the doorway as it was, which is what the author turned down; the English front page served at `/` as well, with its canonical at `/en/`, which gives the home page a second address in the address bar; the language chosen from the browser's, which needs a script and sends two readers of one link to different pages. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/site/ApexPage.tsx`, `web/src/site/render.tsx`, `web/src/site/addresses.ts`, `web/src/site/metadata.ts`, `web/tools/sitecheck/`; SPEC 8.12.2.

**R209. The site's menu has "Research" before "About", and folds below 1100 px, where its six entries still fit in Russian.** *(T72.13.2, decided by the author on 2026-10-05: the page keeps its name in the menu, and the fold moves to the width measured; it revises R202's fold.)* The page "Research" (R204) stands in the menu before "About" and first in the footer. With six entries, the menu fits in a row beside the logo and the tools down to 1039 px in Russian and 976 px in English in Chromium with a classic scroll bar, and down to 1024 px and 962 px in WebKit, at the site's 14 px of padding, so it folds below 1100 px, with the margin of some sixty pixels R202 kept. The width is measured by `just site-fold` (`web/scripts/fold.ts`), which shows the menu in a row at every width over the site as it is built and walks the window's width down a pixel at a time, in the pinned Playwright image; an entry more, or a language more, wants it measured again. — *Why:* the author's choice: the page keeps its name, «Исследование», in the menu as in its title, and the menu folds where its entries stop fitting, by R198's rule. A measure kept as a recipe rather than taken by hand, as R198's and R202's were, is there for the next entry and for the languages T66b brings. — *Rejected:* a shorter word in the Russian menu, such as «Наука», which would keep the fold at 992 px but name the page otherwise in the menu than in its title and its footer; padding of 10 px rather than 14 px around each entry, which saves some fifty pixels in all, too few; the fold at 1200 px, which would hide behind the button, between 1100 and 1200 px, a menu that fits. — *Where it lives:* `web/src/site/frame.ts`, `web/src/site/style.css`, `web/scripts/fold.ts`, the `site-fold` recipe; SPEC 8.12.2.

## The boundaries of v1

**R14. There are no printable task sheets in v1.** There will be no tool that hands the adult 3–5 checked tasks as a single text. — *Why:* the profile stores fingerprints of past tasks, not their texts (О-40), so a sheet could only be assembled from tasks generated on the spot: 3–5 generations in a row, which is the message limit of a free tier, a daily-limit calculation of its own, and in effect a return to the cancelled task buffer (О-6). — *Where it lives:* PRODUCT 2 ("not included"); no separate task is added in phase 7 (О-47).
